# SoccerBotStudio — Service Contracts

## Checkout and recovery storage contracts

PR76 and PR77 are merged. Their successful fresh single-checkout and reconciliation paths each use five D1 round trips; these are separate operations, not one shared five-call budget. Reconciliation claims and loads immutable evidence, records verified payment, separately reserves accounting and attaches its provider admission, then atomically publishes current verified booking observation and confirmation. Read-only provider work uses the existing bounded claim lease; accounting and publication require current durable generation and lease. Prior unknown-write recovery retains release-before-terminal ordering and can exceed five. Definitive confirmation still requires current confirmed bookings and paid invoice evidence.

The current batch-checkout candidate reuses the single-checkout preparation and payment owners. Fresh single remains5 total D1 round trips; batch uses2×sessions+7, including HTTP foundation/authentication. The batch boundaries are foundation/authentication; preparation plus batch dispatch claim; returned batch ID; one claim and one raw/key retention transaction per booking; batch-close claim; raw closure evidence; validated association/hold/payment reservation; final URL save and fresh response authorization. Each physical write retains its own fresh authority predicate. Raw evidence remains durable before strict normalization. Restored, malformed, failed-token and unknown-outcome paths keep additional work; no successful-path budget caps recovery. The measured formula is not a proven absolute minimum. Relative to single checkout plus two trips per additional session, four trips remain for batch lifecycle: returned batch ID retention, the first book claim after createBatch, the closeBatch claim and raw closure retention. Combining returned evidence with the next claim would leave it volatile during possible token refresh, or move fresh authorization ahead of that refresh. Combining raw closure evidence with validated association would lose the evidence when validation fails. Preserve these boundaries in this candidate; further consolidation requires explicit crash/revocation coverage. This candidate requires exact-head CI and owner live verification.

Ongoing confirmation polling remains at most one check per2seconds for the first20 checks, then at least10seconds, with allowance120 and original checking deadline. This task does not change confirmation UI, polling or payment verification.

## Provider token cache contract

The current candidate adds two encrypted, replaceable token rows in the existing account Durable Object SQLite store, not D1. AES-GCM binds the ciphertext to environment, deployment account, provider company, token family, family credential/admin login and original issue/expiry times. Domain-separated key derivation and scope fingerprints never store the API credential or encryption key. A restarted coordinator restores only a valid matching token; cache hits do not rewrite or extend its deadline. Public retention remains50minutes, admin60seconds. Identity proof remains memory-only, at most30seconds and bounded by both tokens. Restoring a token does not restore booking/payment authority.

Explicit authentication rejection invalidates only the affected token family and identity proof. Non-authentication identity/read failures retain valid tokens and preserve the existing account cooldown. Credential changes within an instance fail closed; fresh instances reject old credential/account cache records. Concurrent authentication shares one refresh; generation and invalidation epochs reject stale completion. Fixed provider_token_cache diagnostics identify absent, restored, expired, invalid, scope_changed and authentication_rejected and stale_rejection outcomes with correlation ID and family only. No credentials, token values, provider/customer IDs or checkout URLs enter logs. Physical request admission/rate limits and the no-implicit-retry rule remain unchanged.

## Confirmation read snapshot contract

Protected context consolidates attempt, route, payment, progress and stored availability into one owner-scoped primary snapshot per request. Status reads retain independent authentication and atomic poll quota; successful admission is followed by a new primary snapshot before deciding confirmation. No snapshot survives an HTTP request or write boundary. Scope validation, current booking evidence, stale-proof recovery, immutable verification timestamps and fresh export authorization remain required. Normal observed counts, excluding outer binding lookup: context4, pending status5, confirmed status4, receipt5. These are round trips, not a limit on recovery or failure work.

## Current checkout and queue contracts — 20 October 2026

[Current implementation rules](SOCCERBOT_RULES_AND_ARCHITECTURE.md#current-implementation-rules--20-october-2026) own the design. This section owns its runtime contracts; dated evidence belongs in Journey.

**Confirmation:** HitPay recovery records verified payment identity monotonically. Until current SimplyBook booking and paid-invoice evidence is recorded, protected reads return pending payment-received feedback with no customer confirmation proof. Cancelled, mismatched, unreadable or unresolved bookings cannot publish success. Recovery retains its original financial claim and never replays an unknown payment write. Existing snapshots require observed reconciliation before exposure; older immutable snapshots remain preserved and hidden until current reconciliation succeeds. Valid later revisits retain their verification timestamp. Export authorization remains independent and fresh.

**Queue:** one active workflow, at most24 waiting jobs, with the last4 waiting positions reserved for recovery. Each waiting job expires after15seconds; expiry removes it before its operation starts and returns HTTP503 `provider_queue_busy`. Active operations retain their own existing deadlines and are not cancelled by queue expiry. Recovery gets the next turn, at most2 consecutive turns while customer work waits. Display gets a turn after4 foreground customer jobs when waiting. FIFO holds within each class. No automatic retry or new concurrency is introduced. Fixed-field `provider_queue` logs report priority, waitMs and started/full/expired outcome without customer/provider identifiers. These are initial admission limits, not measured production throughput guarantees.

### Provider capacity ownership — owner confirmed

SimplyBook settings enforce the shared studio across instructors and entry channels. The owner explicitly removed a separate studio-capacity acceptance gate from this task on20October2026. Do not add per-checkout capacity preflight or block this repair on a separate conflict experiment. This is a configuration ownership decision, not a claim that the assistant ran physical capacity tests. Creation-response validation, per-item durable records, partial-batch protection and unknown-write recovery remain mandatory.

Checkout/recovery D1 boundaries and candidate budgets are defined in the storage contracts above; failure/recovery work is not capped by successful-path counts.

### Pending confirmation presentation

Confirmation-route suspension, protected context loading and pending verification share the compact spinning SoccerBot header above the confirmation card. Initial context loading says Checking your booking; a placeholder occupies the card until protected details are available. Subsequent verification/preparation changes the status message within the same visual layout. This presentation never supplies booking/payment evidence, invents details or exposes confirmed actions before authoritative confirmation. Error/recovery controls retain their existing behavior.

### Confirmation diagnostics

Protected checkout context, confirmation status and receipt responses add fixed-label Server-Timing for their operation and D1 work, plus confirmation_http from Worker entry. Operation D1 counts exclude the earlier binding read; foundation_trace includes it. X-Recovery-Trace is SHA-256 of a domain-separated attempt UUID and joins authorized HAR requests to confirmation_trace logs; request IDs retain per-request correlation. Error responses retain partial timings without adding diagnostics to JSON bodies.

Recovery traces bind inside the queued callback and measure queue, claim/scope, HitPay verification, payment evidence, booking-before, accounting, booking-after, publication and terminal-commit wall time. Fixed operation metrics separately measure public/admin authentication, identity, tariff, booking/invoice reads, accounting write, HitPay reads/searches and provider admission. Cache observations distinguish hit/miss/shared authentication and identity work. Completion timestamps and pending/complete/manual-review/not-claimed outcomes let investigators compare successful recovery completion with the next browser poll. They do not claim browser paint time or identify the earliest possible visibility of concurrently stored proof.

Stage wall times partition the recovery run. D1, provider, admission and token metrics overlap/nest and must not be summed into total wall time; parallel reads report individual call durations. D1 counts exclude coordinator SQLite. Logs contain fixed labels, numbers, request IDs and the hashed trace key, never tokens, SQL, customer fields, raw attempt/provider IDs, checkout URLs or exception messages. No new database/provider calls, persistent diagnostics or polling/token-lifetime changes. Token storage and unchanged retention deadlines are defined in the provider token cache contract above.

## Historical contracts and evidence

Entries below retain their dated context. Where checkout rules conflict, the current contracts above and current Rules section take precedence. Earlier provider-only/native-only, preflight and optimistic-success designs are superseded for the current direct checkout. Legacy routes retain their explicit guards.

RPC metadata becomes headers, not response-body fields. Failure outcomes retain partial timings; unexpected RPC failure may have only the server log. Completed traces reject later background updates. No new D1/provider calls or booking/payment ordering changes. Warm/cold single regression remains27 D1 calls; tests cover correlation, privacy, token/provider failures, queue isolation, parallel reads and background closure. ## Local Review and fixed payment-checking contract — 19 October 2026 SGT

Restored editable Review suppresses background catalogue/calendar reads from the synchronous route, before page effects mount; an unresolved route cannot start display prefetch. Revoked access at the checkout boundary routes to protected denied feedback, retains only the local draft with consent reset, and creates no server/provider booking.

Review performs local selection/contact validation; it does not call fresh availability or prepare/create a booking. Checkout owns current authentication, pre-write availability/price/capacity and creation. Exact single-booking creation association/confirmed sessions/unpaid invoice evidence is reusable for five seconds, with a validated due-time; batches, missing due-time and stale evidence use existing readback. Failed combined creation returns the retained pending attempt directly to protected recovery without waiting for a checkout-link request.

Protected context/confirmation checking.deadlineMs derives from immutable checkout_progress.booking_created_ms + payment_window_ms +300000. This is780000ms for the current eight-minute policy; historical policies retain their recorded window. Server status reads retain the120-read cap and five initial fast reads, then ongoing reads are spaced at least ten seconds. Browser storage preserves the original deadline and one final read across reload; it cannot authorize payment/cancellation. HitPay recovery keeps its existing claim/try/provider limits, schedules unpaid reads up to the fixed milestone, and retains authenticated newer-webhook wakeups atomically.

A dispatched HitPay request cannot release the active grant merely because it reports expired, even after the grace window or receipt checkpoint reconciliation. Expiry is not a refund/cancellation API. Unknown and late-settling outcomes remain retained; no new request is created and no checkout URL is offered on return. Automatic cancellation and replacement remain unavailable until terminal-unpaid semantics and verified capacity release can be established. Non-dispatched legacy holds cannot release before both their hold and checking deadlines. Existing protected downloads, stored success timestamps and financial reconciliation remain authoritative.

## Mandatory test-impact pass after every change — 19 October 2026

After every logical behavior/configuration change, and again after any repair, complete one explicit test-impact pass before committing/publishing. This applies to optimizations and presentation changes as well as new features; it does not require rerunning the entire suite after each keystroke.

1. State the changed observable contract and unchanged protections. Identify affected component/domain, Worker/API, browser, fixture/helper and inventory owners.
2. Search repository-wide for old assumptions using names and behavior, not one exact variable spelling: request counts, prefetch ranges, response waits, timing milestones, labels/selectors, navigation, fixture defaults and duplicated inline setup. Inspect both shared helpers and standalone journeys. For a four-to-one-month change, inspect warmedMonths, months, calendarReady, every calendar response predicate and call-count assertion; changing only guestDetails is incomplete.
3. Update every affected expectation to the new contract and preserve its original purpose. Add meaningful success/failure coverage where missing. Never remove unrelated assertions, raise timeouts, add retries or reduce scenario/device coverage to make a test pass. Legitimate request-count changes are documented behavior changes, not permission to weaken duplicate-effect checks.
4. Run the affected non-browser checks after the change. Inspect browser sources and submit the exact candidate to the required CI browser gates; browser execution remains owner/CI-operated. Use the final applicable full code/Worker verification on the stable candidate. Documentation-only changes use documentation/source/policy checks, without rerunning unchanged application suites.
5. Before publishing, record the affected suites, searches/review performed, checks/results and pending evidence in the PR. Local passing results never substitute for unexecuted browser tests. A draft may be published to obtain CI; do not label it ready, accepted or ready for physical testing until all required checks pass on its final commit. Any later change requires a renewed impact pass and exact-commit CI.
6. On failure, retain the first receipt, identify the owning cause, search for sibling occurrences, repair and rerun affected checks. A timeout reported during teardown may originate in an earlier unresolved response wait. Never treat rerunning unchanged CI as the fix.

Required CI remains verify / frontend, verify / backend and verify / live-email with their existing device matrices and strict inventories. This is a mandatory engineering workflow, not a claim that a new automated completeness checker exists.

| Failure journey                      | Required observable result                                                            |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Selected slot rejected               | Return to selection, retain contact intent, zero booking/payment effects              |

| Provider unavailable before creation | Unresolved UI, no booking or payment, retain original intent                          |
| Creation response unknown            | Preserve attempt across reload; never replay creation or enable payment               |
| Created booking readback fails       | Retain provider references; no unverified hold or payment                             |
| Access revoked on Details            | Keep entered form, deny dispatch, zero provider creation                              |
| Successful hold response lost        | Reload restores the same reservation with one creation                                |
| Payment creation fails               | Preserve hold on Review; no false confirmation or slot reselection                    |
| Completed-attempt dismissal fails    | Open first step immediately, retain receipt and exit intent, explicit protected retry |
| Catalogue unavailable                | Visible retry, no invented sessions and no renewed provider calls during cooldown     |

## Completed booking exit and provider diagnostics — 18 October 2026

POST /api/attempts/{id}/dismiss accepts no body and runs in the account foreground queue. Current owner authentication, same origin, verified saved confirmation and a complete ongoing grant are required. Compatible migration0032 retains immutable completed grant history. One D1 batch copies the exact eligible grant then removes only its active copy; repeated exits are idempotent and cannot remove a subsequent grant. GET booking/current then returns empty until a new booking is prepared; receipt details read the original snapshot from active or completed history after existing authorization. The frontend awaits success before either exit navigation and keeps confirmation with an error on failure. No provider call, cancellation, refund, deadline extension or recovery reset.

Provider failure logs contain only correlation ID, fixed operation name, duration and bounded failure classification (timeout, caller_aborted, connection, representation or application error code). Never log raw errors, URLs, headers, request/response bodies or secrets. Existing 15-second call timeout, one-minute cooldown, accounting and unknown-write rules remain unchanged.

## Single-session validation consolidation — 18 October 2026

The Details Review booking operation retains fresh preparation, one attempt identity and holding creation. For one session, orchestration calls the full adapter revalidate once before its atomic dispatch claim. The validated intent is hash-compared before claim and again before creation; current deadline/session-time checks, authenticated effect reservation and native assertCurrent remain. No browser cache, shared TTL extension or provider validation bypass is introduced. Validation failures retain the original prepared attempt and error mapping with no dispatch/effect; uncertain provider writes retain recovery_required under the original identity. Multiple sessions continue preflight(0..n) then validate/create each item sequentially.

Mechanism acceptance requires one validation/create/finalize for a successful singleton, no writes on unavailable/changed-price/failed validation, revoked access blocking effect reservation, one winner under concurrent requests, and no replay after an unknown creation. Existing multi-session preflight, per-write validation and partial-failure assertions remain unchanged. ## Migration runbook and required acceptance evidence — 16 October 2026

This is the owner-requested process for developer and future client migrations. It documents work to perform, not a new authorization for production cutover, provider mutations, browser execution by Codex or payment. Obtain specific merge approval and a concrete deployment/provider-test scope where required by the standing rules. Preserve the three canonical documentation owners.

### Prepare the target and retained history

1. Record the intended company login, environment, timezone, source revision, Worker/origin, D1 and coordinator identities. Map service/provider IDs and relationships from the target account; do not assume equal numeric IDs mean equal records across companies. Verify names, active/visible services, prices/tax/deposit, duration/changeover, player policy, hours, lead time, instructor non-overlap and relevant date overrides. Keep provider-selected starts. Confirm intake/comment behavior, unpaid expiry, manual external-payment permissions and no premature paid invoice state. For the current product: one shared studio,40minutes plus10minutes changeover,1–4players, noon–20:00 SGT, four-hour lead, SGD88 weekdays and SGD108 weekends/Singapore public holidays, player/school comments and no mandatory intake fields. Reconfirm client-approved differences explicitly.
2. Inventory API/feature entitlements and public/admin authentication, company identity and signing input. Verify signing through a supported bounded read of a known target-account record where one exists and authorization permits; otherwise record signing acceptance as pending the first owner hold/readback. Never create a booking merely to test a key or confuse SimplyBook signing with HitPay webhook HMAC.
3. Inventory payment merchant/business ownership, settlement destination, currency, sandbox versus live endpoints, key/salt ownership, webhook URL/events and SimplyBook external processor. An unchanged HitPay merchant is valid only when explicitly intended; a client move requires its own financial configuration review. Verify public origin, email delivery, Turnstile allowed hostname/sitekey, cookies and protected receipt access for the client origin. Do not promote sandbox contracts or hostname allowlists silently to production.
4. Securely prepare one configuration bundle per environment. Validate required names and target without printing values; reject mixed-account input. Install with the supported deployment mechanism. Record non-secret provenance/version IDs, never secret hashes or contents in public artifacts. Preserve old credentials in their original-account recovery path. Verify source and package inventories exclude real .env files.
5. Audit source, frontend embeds, navigation/URL validators, tests, deployment scripts, cached identity/catalogue scopes, scheduled recovery and webhook ownership for old-account assumptions. Classify each as removal/replacement, intentional historical compatibility, or an unresolved obligation. Do not globally replace social branding or historical links/rows. Keep account isolation guards; fix the owning configuration or component instead of bypassing a guard.
6. Establish the legacy ledger and a private, protected backup/export strategy. Record original deadlines and unresolved financial effects. Retain functioning original-account recovery/receipt access where required; document exactly what is unsupported. No financial cleanup is implied by migration approval.

### Activate and verify

1. Freeze the reviewed source and configuration candidate. Require final-source frontend, backend and live-email CI, intended artifact profile/origin and specific merge approval. Record the serving source separately from the PR head; unmerged optimization is not available to the owner trial.
2. Prepare a finite cutover packet: exact target/version, old active version, intended settings, authorized reads/writes and budgets, operator, stop conditions and rollback limits. Stop new old-account attempts through the approved admission control or maintenance mechanism; account for in-flight holds/payments and late events before switching. Do not assume such a maintenance feature already exists.
3. Compare saved and active Cloudflare versions, script/source identity, non-secret bindings, secret binding names, D1/coordinator and cron. Activate the intended candidate only under cutover authorization. A successful settings save or upload is not activation. Avoid mixed-account traffic splitting.
4. Independently read deployment traffic percentages and Worker health/version/source, then verify expected company identity and bounded account-specific catalogue/calendar data. Recheck payment configuration, webhook destination/registration evidence and hostname/identity integration. Distinguish credential presence, authentication, signing, account identity and financial acceptance. Retain unresolved checks explicitly; do not infer client readiness from a working developer account.
5. If verification fails, stop new attempts and diagnose with bounded reads. Never blindly roll back credentials or database state after new-account effects: preserve both account journals, original references and pending callbacks. A rollback packet must explain account routing and recovery continuity, not just name an old version.
6. Run one owner-operated acceptance booking only after readiness. Choose a currently eligible new-account session, verify price/players/details and one unpaid hold/invoice after Review booking, then use that same hold for one payment. Verify exact request/merchant/currency/amount, provider payment and booking evidence, one external accounting effect, authenticated callback/recovery, website confirmation and protected receipt. Stop on the first unknown outcome; retain the same attempt and references. Do not retry/recreate/pay again or infer success from the checkout screen. Additional guest/member, device, no-return, expiry, contention and recovery cases require their own bounded test scope. Live-client financial testing needs explicit client payment/refund authority; sandbox success alone is not production acceptance.

### Browser tests through actual application APIs

Required mechanism tests drive the shipped browser UI through the real Worker HTTP routes, query parsing, cookies/authentication, coordinator, journal/storage and confirmation owner. Do not fulfill application /api responses with success fixtures for this evidence level, invoke only internal handlers, or replace the booking service with a UI fake. Provider I/O may be deterministically substituted only at its owning outbound boundary; retain attempted-call counts and prohibit real external traffic in isolated CI. Identify any substituted identity/email/bot boundary in the receipt. Existing presentation fixtures remain useful but do not satisfy this requirement.

Exercise the normal guest path and relevant member/restoration paths, stale selection rejection, forbidden/expired authorization, duplicate submission, hold restoration and same-hold payment. Assert one booking/payment effect per authorized attempt, no selected-slot availability query after holding, no old-account iframe/request, preserved details on selection conflict, no paid confirmation without independent evidence and protected receipt denial for foreign/expired access. Verify real HTTP route/query behavior, not only component state. CI retains desktop Chromium, phone WebKit and tablet WebKit and all existing required gates. A browser wired to real application APIs with provider doubles is mechanism evidence, not actual SimplyBook/HitPay acceptance. The latter uses deployed application APIs and real authorized provider endpoints, without success-response interception, in the owner-operated test.

### Mutation testing and state-changing API tests

Code mutation testing deliberately changes one critical behavior in an isolated disposable checkout to establish whether the intended test detects it. Include representative mutants that remove a fresh write-boundary check, permit cross-account scope, bypass consent/price/capacity/lead validation, admit a duplicate booking/payment effect, trigger a post-hold slot query, weaken signature verification or allow confirmation without paid evidence. For each mutant record source revision, operator/change, targeted invariant/test, baseline pass, mutant failure and restoration. An invalid or non-compiling mutant is not evidence that a business invariant is tested. Investigate surviving non-equivalent mutants; report uncovered, equivalent and untested cases explicitly. Never lower assertions, skips, retries or timeout gates to obtain a result. No mutation tooling, coverage percentage or completed campaign is claimed by this documentation; select a bounded implementation and review any coverage target before execution. Do not publish or deploy mutant code.

State-changing API tests are distinct from code mutation testing. In isolated application/runtime tests, exercise concurrent/duplicate submissions, repeated/out-of-order authenticated callbacks, interruption after an uncertain provider write, expired authorization/deadlines and recovery claims. Assert durable attempt ownership, retained unknown-write evidence, no duplicate effects, no deadline extension and no automatic replay after ambiguity. Live provider writes, payments, refunds or cancellations are permitted only by the separate owner-approved acceptance packet; a mutation-test requirement grants no such authority.

### Latency and performance evidence

Measure the customer journey, not just endpoint or provider-call counts. Capture at least Details → Review (including hold creation), Continue to payment → hosted handoff, and payment return → verified confirmation. Break down browser queueing/network, account-coordinator wait, catalogue/identity/availability reads, preparation/preflight, holding creation/readback, payment request and recovery/confirmation. Preserve request correlation and original attempt identity while excluding contact, cookies, keys and capability URLs from shared evidence.

Record exact deployed source/version, target company/environment, device/browser/network, time in SGT, test case, visible/background calendar work, cold versus warm caches, request/provider-call counts, errors and observed durations. Compare baseline and candidate under comparable conditions; distinguish browser-aborted work from server work that finishes. Deterministic queued-calendar tests prove ordering/serialization, not live speed. A faster endpoint cannot pass if validation or effect correctness regresses.

Report individual observations and sample size for the first bounded owner trial. Report medians/tail percentiles only with an adequate declared sample and sampling method; never infer p95 or a repeatability claim from one booking. Agree latency acceptance thresholds and additional booking/call/load budgets before a benchmark campaign; do not invent a target, multiply real payments for statistics or overload shared studio/provider quotas. Historical approximately53-second Details → Review evidence is an old-account observation, not a current baseline or target. Fewer calls, a warm cache or passing CI cannot establish measured new-account latency improvement.

### Completion record

For each requirement retain status (passed, failed, blocked, pending or excluded with owner decision), exact source/environment, operation scope, timestamps, safe evidence and remaining obligation. Keep synthetic UI, actual application-API mechanism, code mutation, live-provider, performance and physical-device results separate. Migration is complete only when the agreed target acceptance and legacy obligations have evidence; source implementation, environment readiness and activation are independent milestones. This documentation PR implements none of the new test harnesses or deployment identity assertions.

## Booking-check review and legacy continuity requirements — 16 October 2026

The current request sequence is Details reserve → server prepare/authorizeCustomerCheckout with a fresh selected-day read → native hold orchestration with full-cart preflight and a fresh check before each write → exact booking/invoice readback. ProviderQueue changes only waiting order in the account coordinator. Foreground day/preparation/holding/payment/recovery operations can overtake waiting display calendar/catalogue work; an active operation completes. FIFO within each class and a maximum four-job foreground burst preserve display progress. Each queued operation retains its existing execution-time authorization and freshness checks. Rejection propagates unchanged without retry, and the queue continues. Pending jobs are not durable effect authority: existing journals, claims and fences survive process loss. No additional availability query follows holding. Synthetic order/serialization/no-replay tests and existing HTTP hold/payment/webhook tests establish mechanism behavior, not new-account wall-clock latency.

If infrastructure is isolated, keep the original authenticated recovery/receipt endpoint operational; a fresh database does not preserve it. Validate unknown-write handling and cross-company rejection before enabling routing. Receipt access must use original ownership and a supported fresh confirmed read under the original account; the current closed checking window and stale observation cannot authorize export. No automatic deadline extension, row resolution, external-payment replay, cancellation or refund is part of this design. This is an unimplemented migration prerequisite, not cutover authorization.

## Invoice authorization investigation: session continuity is the specific lead — 13 October 2026

Offline review compared six relevant source files byte-for-byte with tested b2862e0: transport, native adapter, normalization, access, native-checkout domain and protected confirmation. The app requests the documented GET /admin/invoices/{id}/payment-link, validates and preserves its URL, stores it against the immutable invoice association, and supplies a direct anchor. It neither constructs the native invoice-details query nor transfers SoccerBot cookies into SimplyBook. Provider API transport denies redirects; it does not pre-open the payment capability. The current leading hypothesis is loss or unavailability of invoice-specific authorization while the provider creates/replaces its public session across the direct-link redirect. Owner cookie evidence for33 contains two differing values for the same public-session name/domain/path, one matching the failed request. Keep values out of records. RFC6265 specifies replacement for that cookie identity and warns about concurrent response races; this makes session continuity a specific lead, not proof that a race occurred. Request attribution, ordered Set-Cookie transitions and the server-side grant target are unavailable. Normal rotation, backend session persistence/routing, expiry and unisolated browser context remain alternatives. Do not assert double issuance, server fault or browser fault as established.

## Body-independent DELETE acknowledgement — 13 October 2026

The [official webhook contract](https://help.simplybook.me/wiki/REST_API_webhooks) documents DELETE by subscription ID and an active-only default list, but does not specify a DELETE response body. Read-only reconciliation already established the prior empty active baseline after the unknown response; it did not identify that response's exact format.

For webhook-delete only, provider-transport rejects redirects and unsuccessful HTTP status as before. For successful HTTP status it cancels any response body without parsing or buffering, then returns bounded acknowledgement metadata: httpStatus (200–299), contentType (absent/json/other), bodyPresent (boolean). These fields describe transport acknowledgement, not deletion proof, and expose no response values or arbitrary headers. The subscription lifecycle includes this metadata in its private result, then performs its existing fresh normalized active-list read. Exact baseline equality is required before closed/verified:true. A retained subscription, changed prior record, malformed read or failed verification retains delete_unknown and prohibits DELETE replay. All non-DELETE operations retain existing JSON/content-type/size/UTF-8/RPC validation.

No historical grant, subscription, booking, invoice or applied migration is rewritten. The next acceptance test must use one new finite subscription/booking window on the already selected Nabil3/service2, 15 October at/after15:40 Singapore, fresh availability and the ordinary48-hour guard. No payment or cancellation, checkout opening, schedule edits or owner-controlled acceptance is included. Current remote state stays closed at471/640, active0; cumulative management310, Worker133 and deployments32. Any new sealed flight must amend the exhausted deployment ceiling and other insufficient cumulative limits explicitly before execution.

## Native validation diagnosis contract — 12 October 2026

`native_representation_unverified` is an integration validation failure, not a SimplyBook error or evidence that a booking was not created. Latest proof diagnostics identify book/session0 and an independent booking-read cleanup failure, but not their rejected fields. Source now adds private `validation: {field, problem}` to the existing first execution/cleanup/residual diagnostic. `problem` is exactly missing (null/absent required evidence), malformed (unsupported type/encoding/shape) or mismatch (recognized evidence violates an expected equality, cardinality or set rule). No provider values or arbitrary property/message text is copied.

| Field/rule family                                                                      | Meaning and owner                                                                                                                         |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `creation.bookings`, `creation.booking.*`, `creation.require_confirm`                  | Exact item count, flags, identity, customer, service/provider, start, occupied end and signing-key representation; normalizer.            |
| `batch.id`, `creation.batch_id`, `creation.batch_hash`, `creation.booking.client_hash` | Batch creation and per-item batch/client metadata; adapter. A known ID/key remains a recovery candidate even when later validation fails. |
| `creation.session_match`, `creation.invoice`, `creation.invoice.status`                | Final complete selected-session set and supported unpaid invoice; no intermediate invoice assumption.                                     |
| `invoice.*`, `invoice.line.*`, `invoice.lines.booking_set`, `invoice.lines.unique_ids` | Complete invoice/customer/booking joins, money, quantity, tax, deposit/recurring exclusions and uniqueness; normalizer.                   |
| `booking.*`                                                                            | Known-reference readback/cleanup ownership, service/time/buffer, invoice and payment evidence; normalizer/adapter.                        |
| `intent.*`, `association.*`, `context.*`                                               | Invalid trusted normalization inputs, with no input contents disclosed.                                                                   |

For example, `creation.booking.end_date_time / mismatch` identifies the existing occupied-end equality; it does not assert what end time the provider returned. `booking.invoice_id / missing` identifies unavailable cleanup evidence and never authorizes cancellation. Native response schemas are validated by named fields instead of retaining Zod issues. Other internal schema failures remain generic. Customer checkout/status results contain neither diagnostic. Private reasons are caught and persisted before the existing RPC envelope; it transmits only status/code. Scheduled representation failure retains `reconciliation_unsupported`/manual review with zero automatic retry. No new endpoint, table, migration, provider operation or authority is introduced.

## Documented native lifecycle and validation phases — 12 October 2026

For multiple sessions, the sequence is createBatch → book each selected item → signed closeBatch → validate complete invoice/booking association → authoritative readback → native hosted payment link. SimplyBook's [API reference](https://be.simplybook.me/api/doc) describes closeBatch as returning the invoice when payment is required; its [staff support explanation](https://tech-support.simplybook.me/t/575-multiple-products-added-when-booking-with-count/581) gives the batch sequence/signature. The [public booking contract](https://help.simplybook.me/wiki/Company_public_service_methods#book) permits require_confirm=true in a booking receipt. These public contracts do not prove current-account response shapes or payment acceptance.

normalizeNativeSession defaults to finalized validation. Only nativeBookingOperations with a created multi-session batch supplies batch-item: recognized require_confirm may be true or false while item identity, active allocation, session times/customer and key remain strict. normalizeNativeCreation never uses that interim allowance. It continues to require the supported finalized confirmation state and full invoice/booking set; null/missing invoice, changed totals and pending finalization remain unavailable. This corrects a premature flag gate, not an invoice read that previously occurred before closeBatch.

For a single session, handle_invoice remains requested in additional data. [Provider staff guidance](https://tech-support.simplybook.me/t/701-how-to-implement-payment-after-booking/707) identifies that option for invoice creation. No new payment confirmation method, independent processor flow, endpoint or cart cancellation is introduced. An unfinished cart without an invoice remains outside invoice-based compensation; no write is authorized by absent financial evidence.

## Webhook and payment reconciliation task — 11 October 2026

**Owner direction:** finish native booking and webhook technical integration first, then run one combined customer/browser/device acceptance flight. Do not repeatedly request physical tests for intermediate source increments. Keep automated regression, current provider contract validation and exact-source CI during implementation; deferred physical acceptance is pending, never passed.

**Outcome:** a booking reaches paid/confirmed only from current authoritative provider evidence for its exact account, attempt, complete booking set, invoice, currency, amount and tax. Browser return is not required. Callbacks are delivery hints into the existing reconciliation owner, not a second payment authority. Missing callbacks must not strand an otherwise paid booking.

1. **Resolve the actual supported delivery contract (M2, M5).** Inspect current SimplyBook/SBPay account documentation and entitlement for usable booking/invoice/payment events, exact payload, signature input/algorithm/key, event identity/time, delivery timeout, retries and response/acknowledgement rules. Establish whether the actual route emits payment settlement or only invoice/booking updates. Keep each discovered field and account/environment binding evidence-backed. new_invoice alone never means paid. If no usable settlement event is supported, implement supported notifications as hints plus bounded authoritative known-invoice reconciliation; explicitly label the limitation. Do not invent a paid webhook or substitute independent HitPay checkout.
2. **Implement the ingress in the existing Worker owners (M3, M5).** Add only the documented method/path/content type; bound streamed body size/time before parsing. Validate the documented signature over the required bytes/fields, fixed account/environment and any documented freshness policy. A browser cookie or query-string paid flag grants no authority. Reject malformed, forged, foreign and unsupported notifications before provider dispatch or protected reads. Do not claim replay-window protection if the provider does not sign a timestamp. Never log raw payloads, customer details, signatures or secrets.
3. **Persist and acknowledge durably (M3, M5).** Map the event only to already associated attempts/known native references within the verified account. Atomically deduplicate using a genuine event ID, or a documented stable digest where no ID exists; keep repeated delivery safe without dropping later meaningful state changes. Store minimal receipt/correlation/delivery metadata and enqueue existing recovery work before acknowledging. Acknowledgement is not payment confirmation. Handle storage failure using the provider's actual retry contract. Unknown/unassociated events cannot create an attempt, discover customer histories, create bookings or generate a payment link. Out-of-order delivery cannot roll back newer authoritative evidence.
4. **Reuse authoritative reconciliation (M5).** Existing transport/session admission performs bounded known-booking/invoice reads, existing normalization proves all associations/money/state, and the single confirmation domain decides the result. Share the existing claim/version fence with return-triggered and scheduled recovery. Duplicate callbacks, concurrent browser checks and restarts must not multiply financial effects. Retain unknown reads and reservations; no blind retry or falsely marked failure after a timeout.
5. **Close no-return and missed-delivery recovery (M3, M5).** Use the existing scheduled handler/recovery_work owner with explicit cadence, deadline, provider budgets and closure. Recover when the browser closes, callback is absent, delayed or delivered during downtime. Pending/failed/expired/late/unknown payments stay distinguishable. Late genuine payment follows the existing booking-validity policy and cannot confirm cancelled/invalid bookings. Native authorized staff remain the refund owner. Any new durable state uses an additive migration; existing migrations/evidence are immutable.
6. **Prove technical completion before the combined flight (M4–M7).** Tests must cover authentic and forged signatures, payload tampering, missing fields, oversized/slow bodies, wrong environment/account, unrelated invoice/booking, duplicate/reordered events, replay, partial or mismatched booking sets/amount/tax, database failure before acknowledgement, crash after durable receipt, concurrent claims, unavailable provider, late completion/fence loss, lost/missing callback and no browser return. Use synthetic fixtures and supported sandbox evidence, not production charges. Required final CI and server-side provider contract receipts are separate from the deferred physical test.

**Execution rules:** inspect and repair the existing owners; research alternate supported native operations when blocked and document the exact remaining unsupported contract. Continue independent source work while external evidence is unavailable. Do not replace missing evidence with a success mock, widen approval/cost scopes or silently change payment providers. Prepare the exact tested artifact, account scope, finite requests/writes/notification effects, schedules, stop conditions and closure/rollback reserve before any separately governed external activation. Current closed grants do not reopen. No physical test window starts until the technical checklist is ready and the owner is ready.

**Combined acceptance flight:** one versioned checklist joins guest and remembered contact,1–4 players and optional school, single/multi-session creation, changed price/unavailable slot, duplicate submission/restart, native comments, exact invoice/checkout, payment outcome states, normal return, browser closed/no return, duplicate/out-of-order/missing callbacks, protected confirmation and logout/restore. Reuse existing accepted evidence and exercise only remaining cases. Record desktop/phone/tablet versions and exact artifact/account; close all temporary test authority afterward. Full acceptance requires all six native deliverables and this reconciliation task; technical completion alone is not launch approval.

1. Minute0: profileA verifies codeA with Remember me off. Observe verified identity and absence of remembered cookie; sign out.

Submit B's original challengeId/email/code once through owner Network tools: rejection required. RedeemC with consent, then replay that exact successful request once: rejection required. 5. At minute19 or later: profileA signs out, obtains a new guest and verifiesE freshly. Invoke all-device sign-out within five minutes, before a restored session replaces this fresh authority. ProfileB must lose identity. A restored-only all-device request is rejected; do not spend another send to conceal an unexpected outcome.
6. At minute21 or later: profileA new guest verifiesF with consent. Leave idle for over30minutes; close/reopen and restore. Confirm correct identity and fresh guest ownership without old financial capability. Sign out and confirm identity cannot restore; an independent new profile stays unverified. Ordinary time passes on owner device; no synthetic clock is used live.
7. Stop by minute55 and explicitly close/disable. Record statuses, timestamps and redacted server evidence only. No HAR/OTP/cookie/body screenshot upload. Unexpected status, uncertain send or lost time stops forward tests; no automatic replacement window.

Before/at/after90-day expiry, rotation no-extension, invalid credentials, storage errors and delayed-response races remain synthetic checks; physical browser behavior is separate evidence. This packet does not claim every operating system/device or90 elapsed days tested. Live SimplyBook prefill, native booking/payment/return/webhooks/no-return reconciliation and client UAT remain open. Exact matching research remains at the documented getClientList ambiguity gap; no provider contact or account call is implied.

## M3.5 implementation design — 10 October 2026

Status: design fixed before implementation and now implemented in source with focused synthetic checks; final CI and live/device acceptance remain pending. Existing identity/access owners remain authoritative. Remember me is explicit, default off. A fresh consumed email challenge may create one digest-only remembered family with account/origin, verified email, original verification time, fixed expiry at exactly 90 days, current/previous token digests, rotation time and revocation time. A separate access-to-family link associates short verified sessions; no provider profiles, booking records or raw tokens are copied.

- The persistent `__Host-soccerbot-remember` cookie is Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/, with Expires equal to the original server expiry. Cookie expiry is advisory; every restoration checks primary durable state. No raw token reaches JSON, storage or logs.
- POST `/api/identity/restore` requires exact HTTPS origin and an existing valid short guest capability. It restores only identity into that guest's own new context, with expiry bounded by both guest expiry and the original family expiry. It never returns an old guest capability, owner or attempt. GET identity is read-only. Missing remembered credentials return no identity; invalid, revoked, expired or foreign credentials fail closed.
- Rotation uses an atomic current-digest compare-and-set. A server HMAC derives the single successor from the presented token; concurrent users of the previous digest receive that same successor only within 30 seconds. A successor is not rotated again during that interval. Previous-token reuse at/after the grace end revokes the family; unrecognized older tokens are denied. No rotation changes original verification or expiry. A lost response can recover only within this grace; otherwise verification is required, with no guessed retry or plaintext token storage.
- Device sign-out revokes the guest and every family linked to its identity, including a newly issued family whose response is delayed. Durable triggers revoke related short identity sessions and guest access while retaining attempts/recovery rows. Late cookies can remain in the browser but cannot restore revoked authority. Delayed responses may require verification again; they must never reveal another subject.
- All-device sign-out requires a currently valid short identity freshly established by email verification within five minutes, not restoration. It atomically records a subject revocation cutoff, revokes the subject's families and short sessions, and prevents challenges created before that cutoff from establishing new authority. A missing/expired/failing authorization performs no subject-wide write. Future challenges require a new email verification.
- The frontend invalidates cached identity before authentication transitions and correlates every completion to its current operation. It re-reads server identity after verification/restoration. Provider lookup remains unavailable pending supported binding; the owner-selected website contact profile above supplies prefill. Customer-entered corrections cannot update provider profiles.

Additive migration0013 must preserve every applied0001–0012 byte and every prior table row. Deployment/migration are unapproved. A future finite live packet must explicitly revoke every newly issued remembered family and verify restored-access denial during closure; disabling delivery alone does not revoke a remembered credential. Failure rolls back the batch and produces unavailable; no cookie is issued before durable success. Rollback is a closed application release retaining the additive schema and revocation data, never dropping tables or restoring expired credentials. Retention/scheduler activation requires a separate reviewed packet.

Acceptance fixtures: opt-out/no family, opt-in digest-only/cookie flags, before/at/after90 days, fixed expiry through repeated rotation, simultaneous tabs and grace boundary replay, process restart, lost response, logout-before/after restoration and verification, fresh/expired all-device authorization, revocation-versus-old challenge, wrong origin/account/duplicate cookies, storage failure, fresh guest ownership and unchanged attempts. UI/service tests cover default-off consent, request payload, stale completion after logout/account change and explicit unavailable profile. Required owner/CI browser evidence must separately cover close/reopen, two tabs, cookie persistence, sign-out/new device, blocked storage and current contact review; physical devices and provider prefill remain open.

Close settings even when discovery is skipped; expire once, verify separately. A failed expiry UPDATE is not revocation: retain failure and compare actual expiry to the clock. All-row fingerprints retain every column; final evidence reports differences, never normalizes them away. Rollback restores a closed deployment, not old database contents. No automatic grant recreation, field creation, booking or payment follows this package.

## M3.3 durable recovery contract — 9 October 2026

D1 remains the sole identity/attempt/effect journal. `worker/recovery.ts` owns scheduling metadata and bounded internal execution; it calls the existing stored-confirmation mapping and `src/domain/confirmation.ts` decision owner. The coordinator retains only admission/accounting/token coordination. No provider booking database, live reader, scheduled handler, alarm, public recovery endpoint or background activation is added.

| Operation                                             | Inputs / durable precondition                                                                                                                              | Result / interruption rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Existing `prepareAttempt` / `claimDispatch`           | Trusted immutable intent, owner/key uniqueness, expected attempt version and unelapsed deadline                                                            | Same owner/key reuses the original ID; conflict refuses. Dispatch reservation and its one recovery row commit together via the0006 insert trigger. A crash before reservation has no effect; afterward the same dispatch cannot be replayed.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Existing `requireRecovery`                            | Original dispatch version, dispatching state                                                                                                               | Version advances; due work is expedited. Uses UPDATE RETURNING so trigger row counts cannot misreport success. No provider cancellation implied.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `claimRecovery(db,id,claimant,now)`                   | Internal caller; due/expired claimed record, eligible original attempt; claimant16–128 safe characters                                                     | Atomic batch increments recovery generation/tries, leases for30seconds and advances the attempt version. Losers get no claim; a stale booking process cannot reserve or complete another effect. No customer capability grants this authority.                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `completeRecovery(env,claim,result,clock?)`           | Same claimant/generation, live lease and attempt version checked again at commit; trusted clock resampled after validation; counters/lease re-read from D1 | Atomic metadata/observation completion. `observation` requires established immutable association, bounded32KiB JSON, monotonic observation time and canonical verifier acceptance. Confirmed/known invalid → complete; valid pending → bounded deferral; malformed/foreign/unassociated/unknown → manual_review without storing the rejected result. A local deadline alone never becomes provider cancellation. |
| `runRecoveryBatch(env,{claimant,limit,now?},reader?)` | Trusted internal composition only; limit1–10, default real clock; indexed due/expired-lease selection                                                      | One bounded pass, no draining loop. Synthetic read only, at most5seconds per read and5 claimed read opportunities across restarts. Original effects/refs accompany the read; no create/cancel/refund/payment method. Existing valid terminal observations can finish locally. Live/unavailable/no reader → explicit unsupported/manual review with zero reader invocation.                                       |
| `retentionPreview(db,now,beforeMs,limit)`             | Explicit historical cutoff, limit1–10                                                                                                                      | Dry run only: first100 expired access candidates, at most10 unlinked owner summaries. Linked records excluded. This is a bounded sample, not a complete cleanup inventory. `cleanupEnabled:false`; no deletion operation.                                                                                                                                                                                        |

Scheduling states are `due`, `claimed`, `complete`, `manual_review`. Existing attempt state vocabulary is retained: prepared (possibly locally expired), dispatching, observed, recovery_required. Recovery completion is a scheduling disposition, not an invented provider state. Initial dispatch grace is60seconds; explicit failure expedites due work. The ordered partial index excludes terminal scheduling records and avoids a full scan/sort. Read deferrals are5/10/20/40seconds before the fifth opportunity ends in manual review; the code also caps exponential delay at300seconds. If processes repeatedly die during claims, the next controlled pass after five consumed opportunities classifies manual review without a sixth read. Claiming work is never permission to replay the original write. Unsupported unknown associations remain manual; no repair aliases, replacement booking or automatic compensation are invented.

Guest expiry/revocation continues to deny protected HTTP reads. An internal claim authorizes only its bounded recovery decision; it neither renews customer access nor changes owner/intent/associations. Old claimant completions, expired leases, changed attempt versions and older provider snapshots are refused/preserved. Corrupt retained evidence is classified unresolved instead of trapping work in repeated exceptions. SQL/storage failure aborts the batch, leaves recovery evidence intact and stops the current pass. A read timeout aborts cooperative work, releases the caller wait and schedules only a bounded safe-read deferral; a late noncooperative result has no commit path. Adapter labels are internal composition metadata, not authentication. There is no live recovery implementation, even when provider settings say trusted-reads.

Minimum retained fields: access digest/owner/issued/expiry/revocation; immutable attempt intent/key/hash/timestamps/version and associations; dispatch/effect IDs, original references and unknown/observed outcomes; recovery attempt ID, status/due-or-lease time, claimant/generation, attempt fence, tries, fixed reason and last-update time; existing operator proof results and coordinator consumption/reservations. No email challenge fields added. Raw credentials, customer capability values, authentication tokens and arbitrary provider errors are never persisted by this slice. Rejected recovery payloads are not written. Existing normalized confirmation evidence stays subject to its original minimal schema.

Retention durations and disposition for expired/revoked access, terminal attempts and completed synthetic/operator proofs need an explicit owner/business decision. Expiry does not authorize deletion. All attempts/effects/dispatches/recovery/operator proofs/accounting are retained; unresolved records and unknown reservations never qualify for cleanup. No legal retention duration is inferred. Dry-run access eligibility alone is not deletion approval.

### Compatible0006 sequence and recovery handover

Applied0001–0005 are byte-for-byte unchanged.0006 adds the recovery table, ordered partial index, dispatch/expedite triggers and access index; it backfills each existing dispatch into due work without changing original rows. Known completed observations are preserved and may later close their scheduling records through the canonical verifier. Populated tests include immutable intent, revoked access, unknown and observed effects, completed operator proof, association/observation and cumulative coordinator consumption plus an unknown admission.

For a later explicitly authorized combined release: (1) refresh exact account/database/deployed artifact and closed admission/grants, preserve the previous compatible artifact and backup/recovery point; (2) verify applied migration names/checksums, apply only0006 with provider disabled and no recovery runner; (3) inspect schema/backfill and preserved record/accounting counts; (4) deploy the exact CI-tested combined artifact without rebuilding, retaining disabled/end0 and no cron/alarm; (5) perform scoped protected HTTP/migration checks. If migration acknowledgement is lost, inspect actual schema and migration ledger before proceeding; do not blindly reapply DDL or erase partial state. Code rollback retains0006 and every record; previous code can operate with the additive triggers, but recovery stays inactive. Never downgrade the journal or reset budgets. No step in this sequence was executed remotely in M3.3.

### Bounded M3.4 resume point

Reuse existing guest access/policy, D1 identity/attempt ownership, named async service boundaries, safe errors and account coordination. Do not reuse demo identity storage or a recovery claim as proof of email ownership. Architecture already selects **Resend** for verification and SimplyBook for native booking notifications; no fresh provider-selection decision is required unless YS changes that choice. Still needed before sending: exact authorized developer From address/domain and its verified Resend account/region, supported secret binding, allowed synthetic recipient, and a finite send/abuse-test allowance. Sender DNS/verification, secrets and Turnstile setup need their own bounded authority; no installation or email was sent here.

Next implementation should define the actual single-use challenge contract before adding compatible fields: hashed random challenge/code, purpose/email/session binding, explicit expiry and attempt ceiling; atomic concurrent redemption and resend invalidation; generic responses for existing/unknown/shared emails; server-verified Turnstile where applicable, bounded per-subject/source send and verification limits, failure/timeout handling without token leakage or sending duplicates, and a scoped verified session only after successful redemption. Synthetic acceptance must cover wrong/expired/reused codes, concurrent redeem/resend, session replacement, cross-purpose/email replay, brute-force/enumeration, denied/expired/replayed bot tokens, transport/storage failure and accounting across restart. Real delivery to the authorized recipient, provider setup and owner/device acceptance remain separate gates.

## Implemented developer foundation — 8 October 2026

### Continuation: internal multi-session orchestration

`worker/orchestration.ts` supplies protected asynchronous composition through the server-only `BookingOperations` contract. No production provider adapter or HTTP booking route implements it; customer creation remains unavailable. Unsupported contracts or insufficient recovery capacity produce no dispatch. D1 owner lookup and current access precede effects. Each session revalidates the complete immutable approved intent; any price/tax/identity/player/time difference stops. Each physical session/finalization effect has a unique durable unknown reservation in additive `session_effects` before calling its adapter. The reservation atomically checks current attempt fence/deadline and unrevoked access. A concurrent follower or restarted operation cannot create another effect.

Only fully validated confirmed booking references matching account/customer/service/instructor/start/players/money may advance. Distinct complete booking IDs and exact invoice account/customer/currency/amount/tax are required before immutable association; root dispatch observation and association commit atomically. Known returned references survive invalid association. Partial/unknown writes, revoked access, changed intent and stale generation enter recovery without cancellation, reinstatement, replacement or blind retry. Finalization uncertainty remains under its original durable step. Complete association still returns paymentAvailable=false because native checkout is unavailable; it is not paid confirmation. This is synthetic-tested application composition, not proof of supported SimplyBook batching or production orchestration.

### Provider identity operation

`POST /api/developer/provider-identity` is an operator operation, not customer authentication. It requires an unexpired256-bit bearer grant whose digest and exact operation are provisioned through the scoped Cloudflare management path in `developer_operations` (additive migration0002). No HTTP grant-creation route exists. D1 atomically changes granted→running before invoking the account coordinator. Repeat POST and protected GET return saved state/result; running after interruption never replays dispatch. Missing/malformed/expired grants fail before provider access. Bodies and queries remain refused. Customer cookies cannot authorize this operation.

Repository defaults remain provider-disabled with campaign end0. A reviewed bounded `trusted-reads` deployment additionally requires the operator grant. Only public getToken, admin /auth, /admin/company/info and /admin/tariff/current are implemented; no arbitrary-method/URL proxy, booking, cancellation, payment-link or callback operation. Installed secrets stay server-side. Tokens remain inside the account Durable Object with conservative60-second local retention, shared in-flight authentication and durable generation fencing; neither RPC nor SQL/logs receives token values. Unsupported token/challenge/identity representations block dependent reads. Identity decoding requires the provider login to equal the installed company login and refuses the known client account.

Every physical request, including authentication/failures, reserves the existing durable account budget and synchronizes storage before transport. At most2 active and4 rolling starts/second; ordinary64/recovery80 ceilings and campaign deadline remain. Redirects are refused, timeout15seconds, JSON limit128KiB. Uncertain transport retains its admission; no automatic retry or refresh after failure. Known failures apply60-second cooldown. Concurrent identity operations share their read and5-second memory result; restart loses tokens/cache but retains consumed budget, generations and uncertain reservations. Results contain only authentication/match booleans, recognized booking allowance, stage timings and accounting. Booking allowance is not API quota. This is one bounded identity proof, not general confirmation refresh or no-return reconciliation.

Synthetic tests precede activation. Native return/notification, player mapping, complete multi-session finalization and independent-client capacity remain unavailable. Provider responses cannot fabricate immutable intent for historical23. PROGRESS and Journey own actual deployed/provider evidence.

This section supersedes historical statements that all Worker/D1 code is unimplemented. It does not promote provider observations into supported live adapters. Existing src/domain/confirmation.ts remains the sole decision owner; worker/confirmation.ts supplies trusted persisted intent/association, verified access and separately stored observations. Browser fields never set access/account/price/payment authority.

| Endpoint                              | Inputs and authority                                       | Result and failure                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| GET /api/health                       | HTTPS, no query/body                                       | developer mode, disabled provider flag, deployed source revision and aggregate credential-presence boolean; no credential values or authenticated-provider claim              |
| POST /api/access                      | Exact same-origin Origin, no payload                       | Server-generated guest owner and256-bit opaque __Host cookie, Secure/HttpOnly/SameSite=Strict; only digest persisted. Existing cookie must authenticate; no identity prefill. |
| DELETE /api/access                    | Same origin and unexpired, unrevoked cookie                | Durable revocation and expired cookie; future reads denied                                                                                                                    |
| GET /api/attempts/{UUID}/confirmation | Cookie authorization first; then exact owner+attempt query | Minimal existing verifier status/reason, or pending/unresolved missing evidence; foreign/missing both404                                                                      |
| POST /api/attempts                    | Same origin and authenticated cookie                       | 503 booking_prerequisites_blocked; no provider dispatch                                                                                                                       |

No other customer/provider method is exposed; requests containing body bytes receive413, URL queries400, insecure transport400, wrong Origin403, invalid access401. Unexpected storage/runtime failures return503 temporarily_unavailable and a server-generated correlation ID; logs contain only that ID and event code. API responses are no-store. Frontend /confirmation/?attempt={UUID} supplies only a locator; cookies and the Worker decide authority. Return paid/status hints are ignored. It shows Checking until a verified result, polls durable status at5seconds while pending, stops after10minutes or an unresolved/error decision, and aborts/discards old-attempt requests. The existing demo booking flow stays explicitly Preview; this new page cannot create bookings. No debug/test endpoint is deployed. worker/tests/harness.ts is only a local workerd driver.

Guest capability lifetime default1800000ms, accepted60000–3600000; observation freshness15000ms, accepted1000–60000; polling5000ms, accepted1000–30000; checking deadline600000ms, accepted60000–1800000. Environment must be developer; default provider mode is disabled. The bounded operator-only trusted-reads mode above does not enable customer booking writes; unsupported modes fail closed. Local deadline never cancels a provider record. Revocation/authorization starts from D1 primary; protected lookup occurs only after access succeeds. Guest access does not establish verified email or Returning Customer identity.

Internal prepareAttempt accepts only server-approved account/customer/service/instructor/slot/players1–4 and integer SGD money/tax, with sums/overlap/elapsed checks. It is not a public endpoint or a provider entitlement validator. UNIQUE(owner,idempotency key) returns the same persisted attempt for identical canonical intent and conflicts on changed intent. SQL triggers prevent intent replacement. claimDispatch atomically increments version and journals unknown outcome before any future external effect; another claim/restart cannot replay it. Uncertain dispatch requires original-attempt recovery. bindAssociation accepts one invoice and unique complete booking IDs only behind the internal caller; association is immutable. Normalization must prove these provider references before calling it. recordObservation uses a version fence and monotonic observation timestamp; stale completions cannot replace newer evidence. No actual provider-write adapter is wired.

Coordinator admission is persistent, at most2 active and4 starts/rolling second; ordinary calls stop at64, recovery at80, and cooldown/window closure fails closed. Unknown in-flight reservations survive restart rather than being automatically released into possible oversubscription. Internal shared refresh/token-family generation claims persist across restart, prevent stale completion and share a5-second completed refresh;30-second lease recovery applies only to reads/refresh, never uncertain booking writes. No token value or booking data is stored in DO state. The continuation transport above now reserves actual dispatches; the original generic refresh-claim tests remain separate from provider verification.

Migration0001 adds only guest_access, attempts, dispatches, indexes and immutability triggers to the dedicated new developer database. No existing/applied migration is rewritten. Recovery preserves data; disable application/provider access and restore the last compatible Worker version if one exists. A first deployment has no previous version; do not delete journal/resource state to simulate rollback. D1 Time Travel is an owner-controlled incident option, not routine cancellation or destructive downgrade.

Native return/no-return/webhook, player-field mapping, distinct-client capacity and complete batch finalization remain unproved. No webhook registration or guessed receiver is requested. Registered HitPay header signatures and legacy payload hmac must remain distinct when an actually supported endpoint is selected. Provider credentials are installed as Worker secrets by YS; network placeholders are never copied. Historical Card23/invoice23/SBPay59 proof remains historical and cannot manufacture original intent for this new write path.

## Composition and authority — M3.2, 9 October 2026

`src/services/contracts.ts` owns named asynchronous booking inputs, results and stable errors. `services/index.ts` explicitly selects `demoBookingService` for the existing Preview; `bookingAdapters.live` is a separately named, unavailable adapter. No query string, storage value or failed request selects a fallback. The live adapter rejects all five booking operations before adapter execution and cannot restore demo drafts or receipts. The existing cookie-authorized `services/confirmation.ts` and `domain/confirmation.ts` remain the sole protected-read and decision owners; their implemented Worker route is unchanged.

`services/booking.ts` validates input/response schemas and correlations around the named methods. The synchronous `demoBookingModel` in the existing demo owner contains only simulation rules and local receipt mechanics; screens never call it. Its existing rule/assertion tests remain, with separate asynchronous adapter/component coverage. Identity, enquiry and assistant interfaces are unchanged; no new returning-customer prefill or identity flow is introduced.

| Named booking operation | Input (plus optional AbortSignal) | Result and availability                                          | Validation / authority                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----------------------- | --------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| availability            | `{ date, players }`               | Promise of AvailableSlot array; demo supported, live unavailable | Valid date, players1–4; bounded result, valid start/instructor, exact date and unique slot keys. Empty success alone means no slots.                                       |
| checkout                | `{ draft, previous }`             | Promise of ready Attempt; demo supported, live unavailable       | Existing contact, acceptance, elapsed-time, assignment and capacity guards. Validated snapshot; identical ready review reuses attempt ID. No reservation/payment effect.   |
| pay                     | `{ attempt, outcome }`            | Promise of Attempt; demo simulation only, live unavailable       | Schema and current selection; same ID and immutable draft. Ready/declined may advance only to checking. Outcome is an offline fixture, never live authority.               |
