| check                   | `{ attempt }`                     | Promise of Attempt; demo simulation only, live unavailable       | Pending advances to checking; other statuses retained. No provider reconciliation call.                                                                                    |
| resolve                 | `{ attempt, now? }`               | Promise of Attempt; demo simulation only, live unavailable       | Existing five-second deadline, receipt reuse and studio allocation; same attempt/snapshot, valid transition and paid association/amount. `now` is a simulation clock only. |

Stable `BookingServiceError.code`: invalid_request, invalid_response, conflict, unavailable, temporarily_unavailable, cancelled. Known demo conflicts retain their safe customer text. Unknown thrown/transport errors become a fixed message; raw exception details are not displayed. There are no invented live HTTP endpoints, browser credentials, account selectors or price/payment authority. Live support requires a separate trusted server contract and provider acceptance.

`BookingProvider` is the sole availability query owner. It reads each distinct selected/displayed date once per revision and shares the result with calendar/basket; cards and render functions do not fetch. Its in-memory key includes mode/adapter lifetime, sorted unique dates, player count, guest/member and account-email context, attempt ID/status, Singapore clock minute and current demo session identity/expiry. Selection/contact edits that do not change those inputs reuse the shared read; changed dates, players, account/session, attempt outcome, focus/minute refresh or explicit retry invalidate it. A known checkout/payment/status conflict advances the query revision and re-reads availability before continuation; it does not replay the failed action. No persistent availability cache or live latency claim. Displayed availability is not a reservation; checkout independently revalidates.

Query results for replaced revisions or disposed subscriptions are discarded; AbortSignal releases supported work, while a correlation check remains mandatory. The service boundary ends the caller wait even if an adapter ignores abort. A just-selected date stays loading until that date was actually queried. Failure/unavailable/unknown is never rendered as an empty day; the basket retains entries and blocks Continue until validated. Explicit retry re-reads without clearing entered information; no automatic request retry.

Checkout/pay/check/resolve use one synchronous in-flight guard and an immutable captured draft/attempt revision. Action state also belongs to its adapter instance; replacing that adapter cancels old work and releases the new screen from obsolete loading. Success commits only while the owner is mounted, the request is current and the demo member session still matches. Editing during checkout aborts/discards it; payment/status actions block alternate draft writes. Leaving Review or Payment cancels that screen's pending wait. Interrupted status resolution keeps the original checking attempt and offers an explicit status check; cancellation cannot undo an already dispatched effect. Existing provider-side unknown-write recovery remains separate and unchanged. Live writes stay unavailable, so this UI guard is not represented as production idempotency.

Review waits for validated checkout before navigating. Payment exposes pending action state and safe failures; duplicate clicks cannot create another logical action. Error/cancellation preserves drafts and attempt identity. Browser reload resumes the existing demo checking deadline; only demo mode loads/saves demo storage. Preview disclosures, navigation, layout, identity behavior and the protected confirmation page remain intact.

## Implemented operations — demo only

The asynchronous booking contract above supersedes the historical positional booking signatures. The table below records simulation mechanics; identity/enquiry/assistant methods retain their existing interfaces. Their browser state has no live authority.

| Operation              | Current inputs                     | Result and behaviour                                                                                                                               | Failure / authority / retry boundary                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ---------------------- | ---------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `booking.availability` | `{ date, players }`                | `AvailableSlot[]`: date/start, assigned `instructor`, availability and optional `rotationAt`                                                       | Deterministic studio occupancy plus local paid inventory; elapsed Singapore starts remain listed with `available: false`                                                                                                            |
| `booking.checkout`     | `{ draft, previous }`              | `Attempt` with new/reused ready ID, immutable draft, `ready`, no booking and no provider writes                                                    | Rejects locked attempt, invalid draft/contacts/policy, elapsed starts, missing/stale per-session instructor or rotation metadata and final-slot conflicts; repeated fresh checkouts are not a durable production idempotency system |
| `booking.pay`          | `{ attempt, outcome }`             | For ready/declined with a valid future selection, returns checking state and five-second deadline; otherwise unchanged or throws a selection error | Customer UI uses success; adverse outcomes are test fixtures, not visible controls. No card collection or hosted checkout                                                                                                           |
| `booking.check`        | `{ attempt }`                      | Pending → checking with simulated success and a five-second deadline; other states unchanged                                                       | Local status simulation only; not provider reconciliation                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `booking.resolve`      | `{ attempt, now? }`                | Before deadline/no checking: unchanged; other outcomes: declined/pending/late; success creates or reuses local receipt and paid state              | Reuses receipt by attempt ID. Missing studio allocation yields late. Browser storage writes are separate, not a cross-request transaction or provider reservation                                                                   |
| `identity.read`        | none                               | `VerifiedIdentity \| null`; chooses unexpired demo session/local record                                                                            | Browser state is forgeable; never a live identity token                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `identity.challenge`   | `email`                            | Normalized email, ten-minute expiry, unused flag and attempt count                                                                                 | Invalid email throws; does not send email or perform server rate limiting                                                                                                                                                           |
| `identity.verify`      | challenge, code, remember          | Accepts demo code `360360`; consumes challenge and saves email/expiry (12 hours or 30 days)                                                        | Wrong, expired, reused or five-attempt challenge rejects; mutable local challenge is not secure verification                                                                                                                        |
| `identity.signOut`     | none                               | Clears both demo identity stores; returns void                                                                                                     | Does not revoke a server/provider session                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `identity.profile`     | `email`                            | Contact defaults plus only saved name/phone/email, or null                                                                                         | Exact email must match current demo identity; no SimplyBook lookup or shared-email conflict resolution                                                                                                                              |
| `identity.saveProfile` | `draft`                            | For matching member identity, stores name/phone only; returns void                                                                                 | Mismatched/guest identity no-op; does not update provider contact records                                                                                                                                                           |
| `enquiry.submit`       | `Enquiry` record                   | Promise of `{ email, delivered: false }` after validation                                                                                          | Invalid contact/type/message/guest count/date throws; no email/message is sent                                                                                                                                                      |
| `assistant.reply`      | `text`, `booking: Booking \| null` | Deterministic help text and local receipt summary; changes/refunds direct to contact                                                               | No LLM, external tools, provider query, authorization or write; no assistant output grants authority                                                                                                                                |

Supporting boundaries: `src/services/storage.ts` parses versioned draft/attempt data. Version 2 writes use `soccerbot-next-demo-v2`; when absent, version 1 is upgraded without deleting the old source. The former global instructor is carried into each session; unassigned drafts derive it from the demo schedule. Existing attempt ID/status/deadline, booking reference/amount and studio allocation remain intact. Old receipt snapshots are upgraded when read so retry/resolve reuses the original paid receipt. Removed contact fields are stripped. The adapter tolerates unavailable/malformed browser storage; this can discard local state and must not be reused for durable live attempts. `exports.ts` builds ICS text and downloads PDF/calendar from a supplied confirmed demo snapshot; download initiation does not prove readable bytes, device save or external delivery. Production confirmation must be fetched through an authorized capability first.

## Demo instructor rotation and customer disclosure

`demoSession(slot)` in the single demo schedule owner returns a `Session` with its starting-block instructor and optional rotation boundary, or null for invalid starts. Blocks start at 09:00, 12:00, 15:00 and 18:00. `availability(date)` returns 14 positions from 09:00 through 19:50, each carrying that assignment and a shared-studio availability flag. Unavailable slots are not selectable. A 20:40 start cannot fit before closing and is absent. The former common-instructor intersection operation is removed: sessions from different blocks or dates can share one booking.

Optional `rotationAt` is the boundary crossed strictly before session end: `12:00` at 11:30 and `15:00` at 14:50. It does not change the booked instructor; the starting instructor stays throughout. The time card and shared session renderer disclose this before payment and on confirmation. Ending exactly at 18:00 produces no crossing notice. Checkout re-derives each session assignment and rejects a forged/stale instructor or missing/incorrect rotation disclosure. It accepts valid mixed-instructor sets and still rejects overlaps/capacity conflicts. Display metadata confers no authority. Elapsed demo slots are now rejected using the single `isElapsed(slot, now)` domain rule: the Singapore date/start (`+08:00`) is elapsed when start ≤ now. `selectionErrors` owns draft validity, the demo availability service marks elapsed starts unavailable, and checkout/new payment initiation sample current time independently of rendered UI. No live staffing or trusted-server implementation is claimed.

The booking provider refreshes its presentation clock at minute boundaries and on focus/visibility changes. Selection dispatch rechecks current availability. The open calendar retains an absolute displayed month across month/year rollover. Elapsed selections are never automatically removed: draft summaries label each one “Session has started — remove to continue”, the basket retains removal controls, and guarded review/payment routes return invalid unlocked drafts to time selection. Continue stays blocked until every selected session is valid. Paid confirmations and locked checking/pending/late attempts take precedence over new-selection guards; existing status resolution/read-back is not relabelled, deleted or restarted because time passes. A new ready/declined payment cannot start with elapsed sessions, but pending recovery continues under its original attempt.

This closes only the bounded demo elapsed-slot correction. Full M4.1 still needs trusted server time, provider eligibility, closures/buffers and actual shared-capacity evidence; browser clock/storage are not live authority.

## Current data and state model

- `Slot`: date plus local start time; no provider booking ID.
- `Session`: date/start plus required assigned `instructor` and optional `rotationAt`; these values persist through draft, payment, confirmation and exports.
- `BookingDraft`: guest/member mode, account email, 1–4 players, up to 100 selected sessions, contact and policy acknowledgement. No booking-level instructor remains. The 100-slot limit is a current schema bound, not approved live cart size.
- `ContactDetails`: full name, email, phone, preferred contact method and optional academy/school/organisation. Removed participant/guardian/emergency/requirements/notes fields are stripped by schema parsing and are no longer required by validation.
- `Attempt`: ID, reviewed draft, status, selected demo outcome, checking deadline, optional booking. Status set: `ready`, `checking`, `declined`, `pending`, `late`, `paid`.
- `Booking`: reference, creation timestamp, frozen draft, studio-assigned sessions and total cents. It has no SimplyBook/SBPay/HitPay invoice association yet.
- Checking/pending/late/paid lock draft/payment mutation. Decline is retryable. Late displays help without another payment. Paid leads to confirmation. Draft edits clear acceptance and the earlier unlocked attempt.
- Missing live states include explicit expired/unknown/partial failure/cleanup-required distinctions. Existing demo late/pending labels must not be used to hide those outcomes.

## Future Worker contract requirements — not implemented endpoints

Before connecting a screen, document its named request/response schema, bounded inputs, typed errors, identity/capability derivation, permissions, environment mapping, idempotency/stale-write rules, provider authority and actual availability. Do not invent endpoint paths or claim an operation is live until its owner exists and is verified.

| Capability family                           | Required server result / authority                                                                           | Required failure and repeat semantics                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Availability and instructor eligibility     | Authoritative mapped service/resource/instructor times, prices and relevant freshness                        | Validate Singapore time, capacity and eligibility on every applicable path; no selection hold implied                                                                |
| Email challenge / verify / session          | Expiring single-use challenge and protected session; principal derived server-side                           | Abuse/rate limits, replay/expiry, wrong code, invalid/revoked session; no account enumeration or browser-chosen verified identity                                    |
| Approved contact prefill                    | Only allowed name/phone for safely matched verified email                                                    | Missing/shared/duplicate results handled without exposing another person's details; user can correct                                                                 |
| Create/recover checkout attempt             | Correlated booking references and matched invoice/payment association only after all sessions accepted       | Stable idempotency identity, mismatch/stale-request rejection, unknown-write reconciliation, partial-failure compensation restricted to new confirmed-unpaid records |
| Payment status / callback                   | Authenticated provider result bound to the correct account, attempt, invoice, amount and customer capability | Signature/replay validation where applicable; duplicate/out-of-order callbacks; pending/expired/late/unknown and incomplete cleanup; never trust return URL alone    |
| Confirmation and exports                    | Protected read of verified final records and allowed contact fields                                          | Deny unrelated/direct-ID access; no duplicate booking/payment on reload; preserve original references                                                                |
| Enquiry delivery, if retained in live scope | Explicit accepted/delivered/failed/unknown distinction for the chosen server mail path                       | Bounded content, spam protection, duplicate/unknown delivery handling; no claim of delivery from local success                                                       |

The exact supported native SimplyBook/SBPay/HitPay route and entitlements must be proved before freezing payment contracts. The 5 October latency design selects D1 as the sole minimal durable attempt/recovery journal and a separate account coordinator for quota/cache/control state; both now have the implemented M3.1 foundation described above and neither may become a replacement booking database. M3.2 supplies asynchronous UI contracts; the provider-dependent live operations in this section remain unavailable.

## Planned latency-aware live operations — design only, 5 October 2026

These are logical operation names for M2.5/M3.2 contract freeze, not implemented endpoints or existing TypeScript signatures. The implemented M3.2 booking subset is described above; the remaining production logical operations below are still planned. Numeric freshness, quota, sample and acceptance budgets have one owner: Rules & Architecture PERF-01–PERF-07. Public documentation does not certify actual account access. Unsupported operations remain unavailable, with no demo fallback.

### Shared envelope and authority

Requests use named records with opaque `requestId`, bounded operation-specific inputs and, for mutable customer state, `cartRevision` or `attemptVersion`. Server configuration derives account/environment/provider mappings; the browser cannot choose credentials, prices, authorisation or a raw provider method. A public service/instructor identifier is validated against the allowed logical catalogue. Capability/session comes from protected transport, not an email/role in the body. Request IDs are correlation only, not authority or a substitute for an idempotency key.

Successful read results include `requestId`, normalized query/revision, `configVersion`, server `observedAt` and `expiresAt`, freshness state and minimal allowed data. An opaque snapshot ID identifies display evidence only; do not call it a provider revision/hold unless the provider proves those semantics. Preserve original observation age across cache layers. For time-sensitive UI use server-relative freshness conservatively, accounting for round-trip/elapsed time; never trust the customer's clock to authorize a booking. Checkout always uses trusted server time.

Closed failure categories: invalid request; unauthenticated/forbidden; unsupported capability; stale selection/configuration; slot conflict; provider throttle; quota exhausted; temporarily unavailable; deadline exceeded; unknown write outcome; partial completion; cleanup required. Freeze exact code names at M3.2. Return safe user-action/retry guidance, correlation, and retry-after only where meaningful. Preserve internal HTTP/JSON-RPC error classification without leaking payloads. An empty successful matrix, malformed response and upstream error are different outcomes. A timeout after dispatch is never reported as a definite rejected booking.

### Logical operation catalogue

| Operation                    | Named inputs                                                                                | Result and repeat semantics                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `catalogue.read`             | service context, optional known configuration version                                       | Allowlisted product/instructor display data, reviewed price display and metadata version. Shared public read; refresh does not authorize a stale price                                                                         |
| `availability.read`          | logical service, bounded date range, capacity-relevant player/options inputs                | Slots with eligible/assigned instructor, rotation disclosure, resource availability and freshness. Server validates range/timezone/configuration; normalized equivalent reads may share provider I/O                           |
| `selection.validate`         | cart revision, full selected session list, player/options context                           | Matching revision with authoritative derived session/price/eligibility results or typed conflicts. Recheck selected dates, including offscreen ones; no hold and no writes. Only current revision can enable progression       |
| `checkout.createOrRecover`   | stable idempotency key, expected cart revision, full reviewed draft, policy acknowledgement | One durable attempt with state/version and either safe progress, complete matched checkout association, or explicit blocked/recovery state. Same key and same canonical request returns the same attempt; changed body rejects |
| `checkout.status`            | protected attempt ID, optional known version                                                | Minimal current journal state, verified references and safe next action. Authorize every read. Multiple tabs share reconciliation scheduling; status read itself must not multiply upstream calls                              |
| `confirmation.read`          | protected attempt/booking reference                                                         | One verified immutable confirmation projection with current payment outcome where relevant. No success until complete provider association is established; export consumes this same protected projection                      |
| Provider callback ingress    | Supported provider event and proved authentication/replay material                          | Internal hint/evidence handling with duplicate/order safeguards. Verify merchant/environment/amount/attempt. Unsupported or unauthenticated callbacks never directly mutate financial truth                                    |
| Internal `attempt.reconcile` | Claimed attempt/version and due-work lease, no customer-selectable provider method          | Read back unknown booking/invoice/payment outcome, persist evidence and schedule next permitted work. Repeated execution is effect-safe; claims are fenced against concurrent workers                                          |

Backend-only provider transport accepts a closed supported operation descriptor, deadline, priority and declared retry class. It uses the one account governor for every physical call. The adapter parses results; the orchestrator decides whether a write is known accepted, rejected or unknown. Auth refresh cannot silently replay a mutation whose commit status is uncertain. Read sharing keys include operation parameters, account/environment/config generation and required freshness barrier; never share customer records across principals or treat all reads as public.

### Native checkout return and payment confirmation — agreed design

**Intended design only; not implemented or verified live functionality.** The intended final browser destination is our frontend's confirmation route, reached through a supported native checkout return mechanism. The mechanism and exact route binding remain unproved; do not invent a return parameter, change callback settings or assume a browser return endpoint is the native connector's notification receiver.

1. On arrival, initially display **“Checking your payment and booking…”**.
2. Our backend validates the customer's protected access to the attempt, then authoritatively verifies the intended account/environment, correct booking/invoice association, expected amount and currency, paid status and valid booking status. Payment alone does not establish a valid booking; booking confirmation alone does not establish payment.
3. Display **Confirmed** only when all authoritative checks succeed. URL parameters, browser state and a checkout return are untrusted inputs and never establish success by themselves. Planned `checkout.status` and `confirmation.read` own status and protected confirmation; these are not existing `BookingService` methods. The offline verifier below can support their future implementation; it introduces no competing live service.
4. Pending results use bounded status checks under the approved per-attempt deadline and request allowance, with those bounds frozen before implementation. If verification cannot finish, show an accurate pending, unresolved or recovery state. Do not fabricate failure or success from elapsed time. Never automatically create another booking or request another payment.
5. Payment/booking reconciliation must remain possible if the customer closes the browser or never returns. Browser return may initiate a protected status read but cannot be the only trigger or prerequisite for authoritative updating/recovery. The supported browser-independent mechanism remains to be proved; this agreement does not choose, implement or deploy a receiver.

Keep two independent proof obligations open: **supported native-checkout return to our frontend**, and **correct payment/booking updating without relying on that return**. Passing either does not close the other. At this design checkpoint NR04 was paused with14 reserved requests; later campaign accounting absorbed that allocation without executing NR04. This design record neither resumes NR04 nor authorizes provider requests, callback changes or deployment; the active capacity checkpoint and its accounting are unchanged.

### Implemented offline confirmation verifier — 8 October 2026

One owner: `src/domain/confirmation.ts`. Actual exports are `ConfirmationInput`, `ConfirmationDecision`, and `verifyConfirmation(value: unknown): ConfirmationDecision`. Zod validates runtime evidence; TypeScript annotations alone do not validate or authenticate it. The input is an internal normalized contract, not a SimplyBook/SBPay/HitPay response schema or an HTTP request body. The existing demo interface and composition remain unchanged.

**Trusted caller boundary:** future authentication verifies the customer session/guest capability and revocation before supplying access. Future persistence supplies the immutable approved attempt independently of provider observations and client input. Future adapters authenticate reads and normalize complete evidence without inventing associations or paid states. Future handlers enforce protected transport, rate/size limits and safe public responses. This function only checks supplied relationships; a matching object, identifier or `authorized: true` flag cannot prove provenance. Never expose it directly as a client-fed confirmation endpoint.

| Input field      | Actual required meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `nowMs`          | Trusted caller's explicit evaluation instant in integer Unix milliseconds; no hidden wall clock                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `policy`         | Explicit `maxObservationAgeMs` and `verificationDeadlineMs`; no default production TTL or detection guarantee                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `attempt`        | `accountId`, `environmentId`, `attemptId`, subject `{kind: principal \| guest, id}`, `customerId`, `approvedAtMs`, `invoiceId`, summary `money` and nonempty expected `bookings`                                                                                                                |
| `access`         | Same account/environment/attempt/subject binding; literal permission `confirmation:read`; `validFromMs` and `expiresAtMs`. These are results of future authentication, not a token or signature                                                                                                 |
| Expected booking | `session` plus independently approved `money`; session contains `bookingId`, `serviceId`, `instructorId`, `startMs`, `playEndMs`, `occupiedStartMs`, `occupiedEndMs`                                                                                                                            |
| `bookings`       | Normalized observations: account/environment/attempt/customer, `observedAtMs`, `invoiceId`, full `session`, status `confirmed \| pending \| cancelled \| invalid \| unknown`. Empty array explicitly represents absent booking evidence                                                         |
| `invoice`        | Nullable observation with account/environment/attempt/customer, `observedAtMs`, `invoiceId`, summary `money`, complete `lines` each containing `bookingId` and `money`, status `paid \| pending \| unpaid \| failed \| expired \| cancelled \| unknown`, and `paymentReceived: boolean \| null` |

Money is `{totalMinor, taxMinor, currency}`: nonnegative safe integers, total includes tax, tax cannot exceed total, currency is a three-letter uppercase normalized code. Expected per-booking totals/tax must sum exactly to the approved summary with one currency; BigInt sums prevent aggregate rounding/overflow acceptance. Invoice summary and each line must exactly equal those independent expectations. Adapters remain responsible for proving provider tax semantics and converting native decimal strings; no float tolerance or historical SGD88 rule exists here.

IDs are bounded internal normalized strings; they are not credentials, capability URLs or email/contact fields. The verifier requires one invoice and one normalized line per expected booking, with unique IDs and an exact complete association set. A future adapter must prove any grouping/splitting into that internal shape; the shape itself proves no native batch/finalization contract. Multi-session synthetic tests establish only the internal comparison, with no arbitrary cart cap or provider atomicity claim.

Every session field must match the approved intent exactly. Play duration is `playEndMs - startMs`; occupied duration is `occupiedEndMs - occupiedStartMs`. Runtime order is `occupiedStartMs <= startMs < playEndMs <= occupiedEndMs`. This preserves approved changeover without hard-coding40/50 minutes or accepting a changed start/duration. Eligibility, price approval and capacity enforcement before booking remain separate owners.

Access is checked first: `validFromMs <= nowMs < expiresAtMs`, positive validity interval, exact permission/scope/subject-kind/subject-ID match. Failure returns only denied/access_denied before inspecting booking/invoice evidence. Remaining strict parsing rejects missing/malformed/extra fields without returning schema errors or record data. Complete unique expected bookings, observed bindings, invoice joins, session fields and money are checked before payment can confirm.

Observations must be at or after attempt approval, not in the future, and no older than the explicit maximum age (equality allowed; zero means same-instant observation). A historical session is valid if its current access/evidence checks pass. A pending result at or after `verificationDeadlineMs` becomes unresolved/verification_window_elapsed; that clock boundary never claims cancellation, expiry or payment failure. Fully verified paid evidence may confirm after that local deadline. Poll counts, scheduling and production policy values remain outside this component.

| Result `status` | Stable `reason` codes and meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `confirmed`     | `verified` only after all relationships, complete evidence, paid invoice/true payment flag and confirmed bookings pass                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `denied`        | `access_denied`; same minimal shape for missing, malformed, expired or unrelated access                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `pending`       | `missing_evidence`, `payment_pending`, `booking_pending`; only within the explicit checking window                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `invalid`       | `booking_invalid`, `invoice_failed`, `invoice_expired`, `invoice_cancelled`; derived from explicit normalized observations, never elapsed time                                                                                                                                                  |
| `unresolved`    | `invalid_input`, `invalid_attempt`, `scope_mismatch`, `booking_set_conflict`, `invoice_association_conflict`, `session_mismatch`, `money_mismatch`, `observation_time_invalid`, `stale_observation`, `unknown_state`, `payment_conflict`, `paid_booking_invalid`, `verification_window_elapsed` |

Results contain **only** status/reason. Paid invoice with cancelled/invalid booking is unresolved; confirmed booking with unpaid invoice is pending, not success. Unknown status or null payment evidence is unresolved; contradictory paid/payment flags are unresolved. Unsupported native states must not be coerced into these enums. Browser returns and simulated success hints are not evidence. Result classification is a current decision, not a durable financial transition or a claim that an unknown payment failed.

The function reads no network, storage, hidden clock or timers, mutates no inputs and creates no booking/payment/attempt. Durable state transitions, monotonic updates, retries, no-return recovery, customer projection and transport authorization remain unimplemented.29 synthetic tests cover positive/multi-session decisions, every mismatch family, strict data, privacy, boundaries, history and repeat evaluation. They do not prove real authentication, signatures, native return or provider normalization. Rules records the approved narrow dependency exception; full M3.1 and all live acceptance gates remain open.

### Attempt state and durable recovery requirements

1. Before external mutation, atomically create/claim a journal record with customer/guest capability binding, request hash, originating environment, immutable approved draft and version. Enforce uniqueness of the idempotency key in its principal scope. A new key cannot bypass an unresolved overlapping attempt for that same customer/draft without reconciliation. This reduces duplicate intent; provider capacity remains the final cross-customer guard.
2. Record each intended external write and dispatch state before sending. Track not-dispatched, dispatched/unknown, accepted or rejected separately from customer-facing stage. Persist returned provider IDs and any supported reconciliation correlation. Never invent provider idempotency or exactly-once guarantees. If an unreturned booking cannot be identified reliably, stop automatic replay and retain manual recovery as an explicit blocker.
3. Intended workflow distinguishes creating, partial/unknown, checkout-ready, awaiting payment, paid, declined, expired, late and cleanup-required. Freeze exact transitions in M2.5 with evidence. Store provider-observed state plus financial associations; a later-arriving weaker observation must not regress verified paid evidence. Reconcile contradictory newer evidence explicitly.
4. All accepted sessions must belong to the intended account/environment/resource/customer association and matched invoice/currency/amount before returning a checkout URL. Bind any quoted amount/version to the exact cart and reject/re-present changed pricing. A batch identifier alone proves neither atomic acceptance nor a single payable invoice.
5. On partial failure, do not offer payment. Cleanup requires a current positive unpaid determination and applies only to this attempt's newly accepted records. Check races with settlement before cancellation; unresolved cancellation/payment becomes cleanup-required. Cancellation and refund remain distinct, and refunds are native-authorized staff actions.
6. Foreground disconnect/deadline does not delete the attempt or release assumed provider capacity. Durable due time, lease owner, expiry and version let one recovery worker resume after process loss. The scheduler may use the selected coordinator alarm mechanism, but the D1 journal is authoritative; prove wakeup/restart/lease recovery before live writes. A retry never generates an unrelated new attempt for an unknown outcome.
7. Invalidation generation changes bracket writes and reject stale in-flight cache fills. Affected unknown ranges remain under recheck. Different selected dates must all be invalidated. Native-admin bookings remain outside our lock and must be caught by provider authority/freshness, never by a copied local schedule.

### Transport, privacy and contract acceptance

- Live auth, rate limits, admission, freshness and state checks apply equally to initial load, edit, checkout, callback, status, retry and recovery. Frontend disabled buttons are UX, not enforcement.
- Protected results and tokens never enter shared caches or public logs. Minimize returned instructor/client fields, set protected responses to `no-store`, and use named capability scopes. Background prefetch is read-only and never sends customer data to public cache keys.
- Contract fixtures must cover same-key/different-body submissions, reordered replies, wrong account/config/cache key, stale timestamps, expired capability, split-brain attempt claims, coordinator restart, token-refresh stampede, throttle-as-HTTP-200, lost response after provider commit, incomplete multi-session acceptance and duplicate/out-of-order/absent callback. Test-cache bypasses still obey the governor.
- Provider proof and deterministic doubles have different receipts. No exact endpoint mapping, final live cart limit, callback authentication scheme or payment detection guarantee is accepted until M2.5 records the actual supported route. Update this catalogue in place when implemented; do not create competing latency-specific service adapters or documentation owners.

## Required evidence and change discipline

Update this owner whenever signatures, results, error semantics, permissions, retry/version behaviour or live capabilities change. Review any generated Worker/OpenAPI snapshot separately when implemented; no generated HTTP contract exists now. Test adapter contracts using deterministic faults, then Worker runtime/D1 integration and authorized provider accounts. Codex Cloud/CI may run browser checks; YS/client owns physical-device acceptance. Mocks, a registered method, a green build and a provider sandbox result are separate evidence levels.

## Presentation navigation boundary — 4 October restoration

`BookingNavigation` owns public booking and Studio entry presentation. `enter(href, arenaElement?)` performs an internal Next route change; with an arena element it measures the reference scene before entry, otherwise it shows the branded loader, labelled Opening studio for the Studio destination and Opening booking for booking entry. `BookingLink` preserves native modifier/new-tab navigation and already-prevented clicks. Reduced motion bypasses decorative delay. Duplicate clicks share one active navigation; click/Escape skip, back navigation, unmount and the failure watchdog clean up timers and scroll locks. The provider remains mounted across route changes so the hero can reveal the destination.

This boundary has no booking/payment authority and does not change service signatures. `BookingProvider` and `guardedStep` remain responsible for demo journey prerequisites. No provider operation, hold or charge is performed by a hero, Studio-link or booking-button click. The reference's older two-studio and staff functionality is not restored.

## Iteration dependencies — current planning baseline

No endpoint or production implementation is introduced by the planning installer. Existing operations above remain demo-only. M2.5 freezes provider-supported operations and environment mappings only after M2 proves actual account entitlements and the native SimplyBook/SBPay/HitPay route. A public integration listing is not API or sandbox evidence.

M3.2 defines named asynchronous request/result/error envelopes, access derivation, bounded inputs and idempotency/stale-write rules. M3.3–M3.6 add only the necessary durable identity/session/verification records and protected prefill. M4 defines authoritative availability and attempt/recovery contracts: all sessions before payment, same attempt identity across unknown results, and cleanup limited to newly created confirmed-unpaid records. M5 binds booking/account/invoice/amount to provider-authoritative status and protected confirmation; return URLs and browser fields never authorize payment success. M6 owns delivery/export evidence, client-environment validation and diagnostics. M7 verifies these contracts against authorized provider accounts and physical workflows before acceptance.

Document each implemented signature and state/error transition here when its milestone ships. Do not treat asynchronous demo adapters as live contracts, invent provider endpoints, expose secrets or create a replacement scheduling database. A guest checkout also needs an appropriate protected attempt/confirmation capability. Reload, stale responses, duplicate callbacks, incomplete cleanup and authorization denial are explicit contract cases.

## Observed SimplyBook reads — 6 October 2026

These results came from standalone user-run diagnostic shells, not the application service adapters. The client company is `soccerbotstudio`, endpoint `https://user-api.simplybook.asia/`, service name `Kickoff Special`. The developer test company `app404` is separate and uses `Kick Off Special`; matching numeric IDs across accounts proves no relationship. Discover each account's public IDs and validate reviewed logical mappings before production use.

| Operation                     | Observed result                                                                                    | Authority / remaining boundary                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `getToken`                    | Successful client authentication; tested RPC IDs are strings with exact matching response IDs      | Keep key/token outside chat and public evidence. Expiry, concurrent refresh and family/entitlement coverage remain unproved                     |
| `getEventList`                | Unique Kickoff Special, observed service ID 2, duration 40 minutes                                 | This does not freeze live prices, player options, resource mapping or every feature entitlement                                                 |
| `getUnitList`                 | Active public providers: 2 Faisal, 3 Nabil, 4 Caelan                                               | Public discovery is `PUBLIC_IDS_UNFILTERED`, not a confirmed production service/provider/resource allowlist. Strip contacts/internal fields     |
| `getCompanyTimezoneOffset`    | Singapore timezone and +480-minute offset                                                          | Interpret booking dates/times in Asia/Singapore; trusted server time still owns elapsed-slot validation                                         |
| `getTimeframe`                | 10 minutes                                                                                         | Account timeframe is not the business's 50-minute appointment-start rule                                                                        |
| `getAdditionalFields`         | Zero fields in the supplied client scheduling discovery                                            | No proof that player count/service price mapping is ready; do not pass 1–4 players as studio resource quantity without proof                    |
| `getWorkCalendar`             | Company/provider day-off flags and calendar envelopes                                              | These envelopes do not prove intra-day breaks or every working interval. Collect interval/break evidence when required by actual configuration  |
| `getCartesianStartTimeMatrix` | Provider-separated starts for bounded 1/7/31-day queries with resource count 1                     | Exact provider/date/start membership is display availability evidence, not a hold. Revalidate and require provider write acceptance at checkout |
| `calculateEndTime`            | Strict end datetime or observed null; sampled offered starts returned 50-minute provider intervals | It also returned ends for absent starts. Never use it alone to declare a slot available, aligned, reserved or authorized                        |

### End-time result interpretation

- R2 selected 36 cases, with 21 generated cases explicitly omitted under the request budget. Both availability snapshots contained the same 268 starts. All 16 selected starts offered in both snapshots returned 50-minute intervals.
- Ten starts absent from both snapshots still returned an end datetime. Caelan on 19 October: 12:20, 13:10 and 13:40 all returned 14:50 despite being absent; his offered 14:00 returned 14:50. These observations do not turn the service into a 150/100/70-minute session. The calculator's internal mechanism remains unproved.
- Ten other absent starts returned `null` with successful transport/RPC envelopes. Keep null as unresolved; do not coerce it to false, zero, an empty schedule or availability. The documented negative `false` is distinct; it was not observed in this R2 sample. Unknown shapes fail validation.
- Catalogue duration 40, the supplied settings screenshot's 10-minute buffer after, and sampled 50-minute provider intervals are consistent with 40+10. The end result is not independently labelled customer play duration, and read-only evidence does not prove buffer enforcement under competing bookings.
- Display play end as start +40 and changeover end as start +50 under the approved rule, clearly keeping those derived values distinct from provider-returned fields. Preserve full staffing during play and studio protection across instructors. Require exact fresh matrix membership plus the provider's final booking acceptance.

## M2.1 capability matrix — reconciled 7 October 2026

This is the account-readiness contract register, not an implemented adapter or authorization to invoke listed methods. Status **observed** applies only to retained discovery; **documented candidate** means a public mechanism exists; **blocked** means the required account-specific evidence is absent. None of the rows below establishes end-to-end live acceptance. The original matrix reconciliation used **0** provider calls. The subsequent developer authentication/catalogue receipt below used the complete **3/3** allowance; no completed availability discovery was repeated.

### Evidence sources and authority limits

- **E1:** the observed-read table above and [Journey's 6 October discovery record](SOCCERBOT_JOURNEY.md#6-october-2026--client-read-only-discovery-and-boundary-repair). R2 `SoccerBotStudioSG_SimplyBook_Boundaries_ReadOnly_R2_2026-10-06.huben1se.json`, SHA-256 `bff151720f74311d8bae66586003a55ba70c24ac380e76f265b2b82f5f028279`: 51/51 calls, no retries, 268 unchanged offered starts, 16 offered-start samples returning 50 minutes. The original client JSON and quoted PDF are not present in this Cloud workspace; this reconciliation uses their canonical recorded findings/hashes, not a fresh raw-receipt replay. Preserve all earlier failed/corrected receipts. Rules retains the direct-Mac exploratory B1 measurements and unmeasured end-to-end P01–P12 boundaries.
- **E3:** the developer API-access receipt below: YS-confirmed configured company, successful `getToken`/`getEventList`/`getUnitList`, one each with no retries. IDs/names/counts only; no account-isolation, eligibility, payment-mode or checkout/recovery acceptance. The older developer label is historical and must not be used to override the confirmed runtime binding.
- **E4:** additional screenshots below show native sandbox controls, enabled features, trial/timeout and the explicit Ref 19 → prior HitPay charge-ID match. Company/merchant isolation and API booking/invoice joins remain unproved.
- **D1:** [SimplyBook API reference](https://simplybook.me/en/api/developer-api/ref), reviewed 7 October. It distinguishes public/admin authentication and documents common-server ceilings (5,000/day, two concurrent, five/second). Candidate methods include `isPaymentRequired`, `getBookingCart`, `getBookingCartInfo`, `getBookingCartStatus`, signed booking reads and admin `getClientList`. Cart output includes booking items, amount/currency; `not_paid` includes pending. Signatures and method listings do not prove entitlement, native invoice identity, settlement or safe unknown-write recovery. Confirmation methods mutate state; never use them as payment-status reads or manufacture success.
- **D2:** [User API guide](https://help.simplybook.me/wiki/User_API_guide), reviewed 7 October, describes an hourly public-token lifetime. Confirm the actual chosen family and expiry; do not transfer this lifetime to admin/SBPay tokens. Keep all credentials and tokens server-side.
- **D3:** [SBPay integration](https://help.simplybook.me/wiki/SBPay.me_integration) and [payment systems setup, HitPay](https://help.simplybook.me/index.php?title=SBPay.me_payment_systems_setup), reviewed 7 October. The native processor is listed; setup uses an activated SBPay merchant and verified HitPay onboarding. These pages do not prove this merchant's PayNow/cards activation, API rights or sandbox compatibility.
- **D4:** [Accept Payments](https://help.simplybook.me/wiki/Accept_payments_custom_feature), reviewed 7 October, describes configured timeout cancellation of unpaid transactions/bookings and manual handling of late payments. Its native workflow is not a guarantee for a custom API path; client timeout, notifications, read-back and refund access still need evidence. Cancellation is not refund.
- **D5:** [Related Resources](https://help.simplybook.me/wiki/Related_Resources_custom_feature) and [Buffer time](https://help.simplybook.me/wiki/Buffer_time), reviewed 7 October. Resources must be attached to affect bookings; the documented resource modes differ. Buffer configuration includes an end-of-day exception. Neither is proof of this account's cross-instructor capacity or closing behaviour. Do not enable/change features during discovery.

No message to these owners was sent.

### Synthetic proof plan — for review, not execution

These scenarios are concrete acceptance probes, not guessed RPC payloads. Execution budget is **zero until approval**. The revised first-proof section below supersedes the old cart-based sequencing; these broader acceptance scenarios remain requirements, not selected executable calls. The review must bind an isolated developer account (historical candidate `app404`; current configured login confirmed by YS without publishing its value, isolation confirmed by YS in the staged proposal), non-settling native payment route, approved service/resource/provider IDs, field map, test-owned email inbox and notification controls. Never use `soccerbotstudio` merely because it is accessible. Broader adverse/cancel/partial proofs need separate method budgets. For the first supervised happy path use the staged proposal below; manual reconciliation and no automatic cancellation replace an atomic-cancel prerequisite.

Use one run ID and stable attempt IDs per scenario, recorded before dispatch with request hash and account/config identity. Choose a future Singapore date D with supported 09:00, 09:50, 10:40 and 11:30 starts; a second date D+1 for mixed sessions. If those fixtures are not offered for the approved providers, stop and revise the plan rather than force availability. Declare 1-player and 4-player carts consuming one studio each, approved SGD totals and fictitious contact data in an owner-controlled inbox. Never send to arbitrary phone/email recipients.

Unused scenarios do not count as passed. This pilot is feasibility evidence only, not the PERF-05 acceptance cohorts or a production latency verdict.

### Native checkout research — 7 October 2026

**Outcome:** official guidance supports an invoice-linked SimplyBook payment page that hands the customer to the configured processor. It does not require a separately invented checkout-creation API. The best evidenced candidate is public JSON-RPC booking with `additional.handle_invoice = true`, followed by a SimplyBook Admin REST invoice payment link and invoice read-back. Exact wire schemas and account rights remain discovery objectives; the staged proposal below uses conservative manual reconciliation without claiming provider idempotency. It is not approved or executed. No provider-account calls were made in this research task; original usage remains **3/3 exhausted**.

#### Research corrections and source authority — D15–D20

All retrieved sources below were checked on 7 October 2026. Staff guidance is not this account's execution evidence; customer examples are not current schemas.

- **D15 — [REST authentication, 28 September 2026](https://tech-support.simplybook.me/t/password-based-rest-admin-api-login-after-ip-verification-was-enabled/2375):** staff explicitly supports the user login plus API User Key in the password field of `/admin/auth` (also `getUserToken`). First User API Key creation permanently enables email/IP verification for password API authentication; revocation does not undo it. Existing key use is not authorization to create one. Key auth avoids email/IP verification, not a blanket promise to bypass every challenge. Password REST authentication may return `require2fa`, `allowed2fa_providers` and `auth_session_id`; `/admin/auth/2fa` accepts company/session_id/code/type email and successful verification trusts that IP. Exact token envelope/expiry and account permissions remain to observe. No settings are changed.
- **D16 — [payment methods, 5 July–8 August 2019](https://tech-support.simplybook.me/t/399-process-to-make-a-book-and-make-the-payment-confirm/403):** migrated staff replies deprecate `isPaymentRequired`, `confirmBookingPayment`, `validatePayment` and `getPaymentProcessorConfig` for the newer payment system. Continued reference listing is conflicting documentation, not evidence of restored support. Remove `isPaymentRequired` from selected preflight; no runtime test of obsolete methods. Later replies support public `handle_invoice`; manual admin payment-confirmation examples are excluded from the native proof.
- **D17 — [batch finalization, 24 September 2020](https://tech-support.simplybook.me/t/575-multiple-products-added-when-booking-with-count/581):** staff describes createBatch/book/closeBatch and returned bookings plus invoice. The customer's unanswered concern about partial capacity is not a guarantee. The sample cart belongs to the customer, not a validated modern schema.
- **D18 — [batch/group clarification, 7 May–24 June 2026](https://tech-support.simplybook.me/t/verifying-api-booking-flow-with-member-discount-via-promo-code/1763):** staff says batch bookings hold slots until timeout if unconfirmed; group booking creates N booking records with one invoice line, and group and multiple-booking modes cannot be combined. This does not prove SoccerBot shared-resource enforcement or atomic batches. One studio session for 1–4 players still uses one allocation; no count=players conversion.
- **D19 — [native confirmation page](https://tech-support.simplybook.me/t/payment-process-with-custom-front-end/1777):** YS supplies the staff finding that the standard processor flow cannot skip SimplyBook's intermediate confirmation page. Direct page and post retrieval failed in this research tool; search confirms the topic listing, not the reply text. Label this as user-supplied guidance pending independent retrieval. The plan retains the native confirmation/payment page consistent with D7; no direct-gateway shortcut is assumed.
- **D20 — [Client Login, 29 September 2026](https://tech-support.simplybook.me/t/client-authorization-required/2384):** staff says public `book` requires client authentication when Client Login is enabled; adding an admin token does not satisfy it. Public alternative uses `client_id`/`client_sign`; additional fields are a keyed object and time uses HH:MM:SS. Admin booking has a different signature and booking-management permissions. Do not silently switch families or disable Client Login. Registration would be an extra write and is excluded; bind an existing synthetic client if needed.

#### Operations and reference flow

**P authentication:** JSON-RPC 2.0 POST `https://user-api.simplybook.me/login`, method `getToken`, params `[companyLogin, apiKey]`; result is a token. Public RPC calls use POST `https://user-api.simplybook.me/`, `X-Company-Login`, `X-Token` and a matching request/response `id`. These three access/catalogue calls alone were executed previously (E3). D2 describes a one-hour public-token lifetime; no old token is retained or reused by this task.

**A authentication candidate:** D8's indexed REST contract uses POST `https://user-api-v2.simplybook.me/admin/auth` with `{company, login, password}` and a `TokenEntity`; subsequent requests use `X-Company-Login` and `X-Token` from that REST authentication. Optional `/admin/auth/2fa`, `/admin/auth/sms` and `/admin/auth/refresh-token` add requests and, for SMS, a notification effect. D15 now explicitly confirms API User Keys in REST `/admin/auth` as the password value. First-key creation has persistent account-security effects; existing-key use is distinct from creation. D8 lists `AccessDenied`/`BadRequest` for auth; exact token/challenge fields and expiry still require the current schema. Do not substitute a public RPC token for admin authentication. D15 explicitly permits the REST-obtained admin token in JSON-RPC; that does not authorize extra RPC operations. No admin credential, permission or call is authorized here.

Mixed-session partial acceptance/shared capacity remain later proofs, not prerequisites for this happy path.

A network-secret placeholder is suitable for outbound login substitution but cannot be hashed locally as the signing secret. Define a separate supported local secret binding only after method selection and authorization; do not request/install new secrets now. #### Status, expiry and recovery interpretation

| Provider observation                                    | Permitted interpretation / next action                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Legacy `not_paid`                                       | Unpaid **or pending**; never sufficient proof for race-safe cancellation.                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Legacy `cancel` / `error`                               | Payment cancellation / validation error, respectively. Neither proves refunded funds, cancelled booking or absence of a late settlement.                                                                                                                                             |
| Legacy `paid`                                           | Cart reports paid; modern invoice/merchant/booking/amount association and settlement provenance still required.                                                                                                                                                                      |
| REST booking's indexed `invoice_status`                 | Candidate values are `deleted`, `new`, `pending`, `cancelled`, `cancelled_by_timeout`, `error`, `paid`. This is richer than cart status but the current schema/HitPay mapping is not account-confirmed. Do not synthesize those states from the coarse cart strings.                 |
| Local deadline elapsed                                  | Observation timed out; report unknown/pending. Do not infer provider expiry. D4 documents native timeout cancellation; E4 displays 10 minutes, while D4 recommends at least one hour. Preserve the setting and ask how it applies to the API-created order. No configuration change. |
| Late payment / contradictory booking and invoice states | Retain paid evidence and unresolved capacity separately; native operator reviews. Never automatically reinstate a cancelled/overlapping session or turn payment into a refund.                                                                                                       |
| Callback or return URL                                  | Notification/hint only until authenticity, replay protection and invoice read-back are verified. No callback interception/forwarding or custom processor is proposed.                                                                                                                |
| Lost `book` or link response / duplicate submit         | Preserve original attempt and dispatched state. No replay, new attempt or guessed lookup. No documented idempotency key or deterministic no-ID recovery was established by this research. Native operator must reconcile under the agreed procedure before another attempt.          |

YS is the confirmed reconciliation owner. The staged procedure below uses a unique synthetic marker, exact slot and preserved dispatch record plus native dashboard joins; no provider atomic-cancel/idempotency guarantee is required for this supervised test. Before writing, stop if YS cannot identify the attempt reliably in native views. Timestamp/contact similarity alone is not unique correlation. A status-check-then-cancel sequence is not atomic: payment may arrive between requests. Stop rather than cancelling on `not_paid`, booking colour, a local timeout or an unmatched ID. Existing Ref 19/20 remains untouched; cancellation never refunds.

#### Stage 0 — offline binding, zero provider requests

Bind one private manifest to source SHA, account/environment references, approval, run/attempt ID, future Singapore date/time (at least 48 hours after planned execution), service 2 and one eligible provider (observed candidates 2 Faisal, 3 Nabil, 4 Caelan), 40-minute session plus 10-minute changeover, one player and **one booking allocation**. Bind configured SGD gross amount/tax, exact synthetic name/email/phone, all required field keys/answers, and current Client Login setting. Price 88 in E2 is historical, not a fixture default. Keep secrets, contact data, hashes and payment capabilities private.

Choose public `book` with `count = 1`. With Client Login off, use approved synthetic name/email/phone. With it on, use a positively identified existing test client's `client_id` and locally computed `client_sign` per D20; do not also pass name/email/phone. Bind client ID/hash and local signing secret privately. No client registration, search, settings change or Admin-book fallback is included. If that existing client cannot be bound, the write stage stops; discovery can proceed. Additional fields form an object keyed by field name, with `handle_invoice: true`; time is `HH:MM:SS`.

Supply one **positively identified SimplyBook invoice ID** from an existing test booking, with a redacted native booking/invoice cross-reference. Ref 19 is an SBPay reference, not automatically this ID. No guessed ID or broad list/search is included. Existing E2 paid records are read-only; do not generate their payment links or pay them again.

Select admin authentication before dispatch: existing API User Key in the REST password field (preferred only if already available), or existing password with the D15 email challenge branch. Bind user permissions to invoice read/link operations. No first-key creation, account-setting change, SMS challenge, refresh or fallback is included. Maximum two admin-auth requests; an unexpected challenge stops. Email verification can send an email and trust the execution IP after successful confirmation; approve that effect when selecting this branch. API User Keys avoid email/IP verification per D15, not necessarily all independent authentication challenges. The current environment exposes only the public API key/company; admin credentials, local signing-secret access and REST-host network permission need explicit managed bindings before use. Do not put their values in chat/PRs.

Reserve 16 server requests against actual available API quota, with reset/other consumers accounted for. The screenshot's 30 bookings is not API quota. YS remains available for checkout and dashboard inspection; record the execution window and a same-session handoff deadline. Use one unique synthetic name marker (and controlled email alias where supported), exact slot/provider and dispatch timestamp. Confirm YS can locate this marker in native booking/client/invoice views before permitting `book`; if the native views cannot distinguish this attempt reliably, **that inability is the write blocker**, not missing atomic cancellation.

**Ceiling = discovery 3 + booking/checkout server path 11 + recovery 2 = 16.** One-step admin auth reduces actual use to 15; stop early whenever possible. No unused allowance transfers to other operations. Server business-write ceiling is one `book` plus one potentially stateful link call; booking may create more than one associated native record. Separate YS ceiling is one sandbox submission. No automatic cancel/refund, manual-paid/confirm mutation, batch, second booking or second payment. If credentials expire between stages, stop; another auth is not hidden inside this ceiling.

D2 may establish enough schema for later reads without support. After B4, parse the private response offline and bind observed field paths before B5/B6. These are covered stage gates, not new permission prompts. If actual semantics require an additional operation or account change, stop that branch and propose a concrete amendment; approval of 16 calls is not approval to invent methods. A failed link entitlement experiment can leave one unpaid test booking for native timeout/reconciliation; this known possibility must be accepted before B4.

#### Conservative recovery and success evidence

Before B4 persist an exclusive attempt journal with source/account/fixture identity, unique synthetic marker, Singapore slot/provider, UTC and Singapore dispatch timestamps, request hash (no credentials), quota counters and `prepared`/`dispatched` state. Write `dispatched` durably before transport. Preserve the first response or error and all returned IDs privately. Restart refuses any dispatched booking/link step, even if no response was saved; no local lock or journal is claimed to give provider idempotency.

On unknown booking/link/payment outcome, stop immediately. Use only R reads whose references are already known. Give YS a private inspection sheet with attempt marker, dispatch window, selected service/provider/slot, amount, returned booking ID/code, invoice ID, SBPay reference and HitPay ID (each explicitly **unknown** if absent). YS checks SimplyBook calendar/bookings and client details for the exact marker and slot; opens associated invoice/transaction from that record; checks SBPay status/payment-system transaction ID; then matches that ID in HitPay sandbox and records status, gross amount, currency and timestamps. Compare returned references, not timestamps alone. If duplicates or no unique match exist, mark unresolved and do not start another attempt. No automated broad scan or guessed Ref 19 lookup.

YS records record disposition and unresolved follow-up deadline before the session ends. Observe existing native 10-minute timeout without changing it; local elapsed time or absence in one dashboard does not prove failure/cancellation or absence of late settlement. Paid-but-cancelled/late/inconsistent results remain unresolved for native review; do not reinstate capacity, cancel or refund automatically. If YS becomes unavailable, leave the durable handoff and stop. A subsequent attempt requires the first to be resolved, not merely budget remaining.

Success requires the redacted dispatch receipt, one valid booking and exact invoice line join, configured amount/currency, native payment-link provenance, authoritative invoice payment fields matched to SBPay/HitPay sandbox settlement and final valid booking. Dashboard corroboration does not turn a missing API field into an API guarantee. Record notification receipt separately. Outcome may be partial if API settlement provenance or updates cannot be established. This proves only one developer happy path; lost-response fault injection, duplicate/late/failed/expired paths, multi-session atomicity/capacity, production and devices remain later proofs.

#### Offline validation before an executable probe

No network-capable probe is shipped or run in this preparation task. Before future dispatch, the bound probe must pass network-disabled transport-fixture tests: mismatched RPC IDs/errors/malformed JSON; missing or ambiguous invoice/booking joins; wrong account/amount/currency; unexpected Client Login/2FA; missing/placeholder signing secret; non-native/insecure links; quota exhaustion; token expiry; and timeout before/after `book` dispatch. Assert that unknown outcomes preserve the journal, refuse replay after restart, permit only the two named known-ID reads, never cancel/refund, and never exceed 16 calls/one booking/one link. Test early terminal polling and all-three-poll accounting; mock-paid results must remain labelled fixtures, not provider evidence. Validate actual discovered DTO paths offline before advancing stages. Do not alter production adapters or weaken the application suites.

**Exact remaining user inputs:** future Singapore slot and selected instructor; configured SGD amount/tax; private synthetic name/email/phone and required field answers; actual Client Login setting and existing test client credentials by secure reference if enabled; one positively identified existing SimplyBook invoice reference/native booking join; chosen existing admin auth mode/user and secure bindings, local signing-secret binding, REST network readiness; quota reserve/reset/other consumers and the supervised execution window/deadline. These are values/access facts, not six technical support answers. Owner isolation, intended structure, inbox ownership and YS reconciliation responsibility are already accepted.

**Approval request:** approve D+B+R together with the bound inputs, maximum 16 server requests, one booking, one potentially stateful native link and one YS sandbox submission; zero automatic cancel/refund and no settings/key creation. This request is required by YS's explicit instruction that provider-account requests and booking/payment execution need approval of the concrete experiment. Until approval and bindings exist, only offline preparation is authorized.
